<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> Let <i>X</i> be a smooth projective complex threefold with trivial canonical bundle. We construct numerical Bridgeland stability conditions with the exact ordinary and square-root-Todd central charges at every sufficiently large volume. One volume threshold works on an open set of real twists and ample directions. The resulting stability conditions have the support property on the full numerical Grothendieck group and stable point sheaves. Separately, on every smooth projective complex threefold we prove a strong tilt inequality above a volume threshold uniform in the object and twist along a fixed polarization.

- **claim-exact-charges-real-sector** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-geometric-sector)): Let $X$ be a smooth projective complex threefold with $K_X\simeq\mathcal O_X$. For any $B_*\in N^1(X)_{\mathbb R}$ and $H_*\in\operatorname{Amp}(X)$ there are open neighborhoods $V$ of $B_*$ and $W$ of $H_*$ and one $T>0$ such that, for all $B\in V$, $H\in W$ and $t\geq T$, the following hold.* 1. *$(Z_{B,tH},\mathcal A_{B,tH})$ is a geometric numerical stability condition with support on $K_{\mathrm{num}}(X)_{\mathbb R}$.* 2. *There is a geometric numerical stability condition, with support on the same full numerical space, whose charge is exactly $$Z^{\mathrm{LV}}_{B,tH}(E) =-\int_Xe^{-B-itH}\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}(E)\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{td}}(X)}, \qquad \sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{td}}(X)}=1+\frac{c_2(X)}{24}.$$ Its heart is the one constructed in Section <a href="#sec:ambient" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:ambient">2</a>.
  - Explanation: The prescribed ordinary and square-root-Todd charges admit geometric numerical stability conditions throughout a common open real sector beyond a common volume threshold. Full numerical support holds for both charges; the ordinary charge has the standard double-tilt heart.
- **claim-uniform-strong-tilt-inequality** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-uniform-inequality)): Let $X$ be a smooth projective complex threefold, $H$ an ample integral divisor, and $B_0\in N^1(X)_{\mathbb Q}$. There are constants $k\in\mathbb Q_{\geq0}$ and $R_*>0$, depending only on $(X,H,B_0)$, such that every finite-slope $\nu_{a,b}$-semistable $E\in\mathcal B_{H,B_0+bH}$ with $\nu_{a,b}(E)=0$ satisfies $$\label{eq:intro-corrected} \mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_3^{B_0+bH}(E) \leq \left(\frac{a^2}{6}+k\right)H^2\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_1^{B_0+bH}(E) \qquad(a>0,\ b\in\mathbb R).$$ For the same fixed data, the stronger bound $$\label{eq:intro-tail} \mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_3^{B_0+bH}(E) \leq \frac{a^2}{6}H^2\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_1^{B_0+bH}(E)$$ holds whenever $a>R_*$, for every $b\in\mathbb R$.
  - Explanation: A rational correction bounds the third twisted character of finite-slope, slope-zero tilt-semistable objects at every positive volume. Beyond a threshold the correction disappears. Both constants depend on the fixed polarized geometry and rational transverse twist, rather than on the object or the scalar twist.
- **claim-quadratic-parabolic-tail** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-quadratic-tail)): For every polarized smooth projective threefold $(X,H)$ there is $R_*>0$ such that, if $$\alpha>f(\beta):=\frac{\beta^2+R_*^2}{2},$$ then $Q_{\alpha,\beta}(v_H(E))\geq0$ for every nonzero semistable object in $\mathcal B_{H,\beta H}$ for the slope $$\frac{v_2-\beta v_1+(\beta^2-\alpha)v_0}{v_1-\beta v_0}, \qquad v_H(E)=(H^3\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_0(E),H^2\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_1(E),H\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_2(E),\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_3(E)),$$ again interpreted as $+\infty$ at zero denominator.
  - Explanation: The uncorrected tail implies a nonnegative quadratic form on ordinary Chern vectors above a parabolic boundary. The conclusion covers all semistable slopes, including the zero-denominator case.
- **claim-full-numerical-product-embedding** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-full-numerical-embedding)): Let $X$ be a smooth projective threefold. Rational Chern characters identify its numerical Grothendieck group with $$\label{eq:full-numerical-lattice} K_{\mathrm{num}}(X)_\mathbb Q\simeq \mathbb Q\oplus N^1(X)_\mathbb Q\oplus N_1(X)_\mathbb Q\oplus\mathbb Q.$$ Products of numerical divisor classes span this space. Given a real ample class $H_*$, there are very ample line bundles $L_1,\ldots,L_q$ whose classes form a basis of $N^1(X)_\mathbb R$ and positive numbers $s_1,\ldots,s_q$ with $H_*=\sum_hs_hL_h$. For the product embedding $$\label{eq:product-embedding} i:X\hookrightarrow Y=\prod_{h=1}^q\mathbb P^{d_h}, \qquad d_h=h^0(X,L_h)-1,$$ the pushforward $i_*:K_{\mathrm{num}}(X)_\mathbb R\to K_{\mathrm{num}}(Y)_\mathbb R$ is injective.
  - Explanation: Divisor products span the numerical character space of a smooth projective threefold. A product embedding built from a very ample divisor basis therefore preserves every numerical class under pushforward.
- **claim-elliptic-volume-scaled-support** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-elliptic-scaled-support)): Let $C$ be an elliptic curve, let $n\ge1$, and fix positive rational numbers $\rho_1,\ldots,\rho_n$. For rational $R\ge1$, put $w_r=R\rho_r$ and let $H_r$ be the point-divisor class from the $r$th factor of $C^n$. The product stability condition with charge $$Z_{\mathbf w}(u)=-\int_{C^n}e^{-i\sum_rw_rH_r}\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}(u)$$ has support on the coordinate lattice $$x_I(u)=H_I\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_{n-\|I\|}(u),\qquad H_I=\prod_{r\in I}H_r,\qquad I\subset\{1,\ldots,n\}.$$ More precisely, set $y_I=(\prod_{r\in I}w_r)x_I$. In these coordinates, $$Z_{\mathbf w}(u)=\widehat Z_n(y(u)),\qquad \widehat Z_n(y)=-\sum_I(-i)^{\|I\|}y_I.$$ For a fixed Euclidean norm there is $C_n>0$, independent of $R$, such that $$\\|y(u)\\|\le C_n\|Z_{\mathbf w}(u)\| \qquad(u\text{ the class of a semistable object}).$$ The constants can be chosen uniformly when the positive ratios $\rho_r$ vary in a compact set. Point sheaves are stable of phase $1$.
  - Explanation: Scaling each elliptic-product coordinate by its corresponding product of weights makes the charge a fixed linear map and gives a support constant independent of the volume. Uniformity persists on compact sets of positive weight ratios.
- **claim-weighted-projective-ambient-support** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-weighted-ambient)): Let $Y=\prod_{h=1}^q\mathbb P^{d_h}$, put $\xi_h=c_1(\mathcal O_h(1))$, and fix $s_h>0$. There is a continuous family of numerical stability conditions $(U_R,\mathcal R_R)$ for $R\ge1$ with $$U_R(u)=-\int_Ye^{-iR\sum_hs_h\xi_h}\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}(u).$$ All point sheaves are stable of phase $1$. Write $\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}(u)=\sum_{\mathbf j}u_{\mathbf j}\xi^{\mathbf j}$, where $0\le j_h\le d_h$, and set $$\label{eq:ambient-scaled-coordinates} (M_Ru)_{\mathbf j}= u_{\mathbf j}\prod_h(Rs_h)^{d_h-j_h}.$$ For a fixed Euclidean norm there is $D>0$, independent of $R$, such that $$\label{eq:ambient-support} \\|M_Ru\\|\le D\|U_R(u)\|\qquad (u\text{ the class of an }\mathcal R_R\text{-semistable object}).$$ For every $\mathbf e\in\mathbb Z^q$, $$\label{eq:ambient-twist-distance} d(\mathcal R_R,\mathcal R_R\otimes\mathcal O_Y(\mathbf e)) \le\sum_h\frac{d_h\|e_h\|}{\pi Rs_h}.$$
  - Explanation: Products of projective spaces carry a continuous weighted exponential-charge family with stable points, volume-independent support in scaled coordinates, and a quantitative phase-distance bound for integral line-bundle twists.
- **claim-geometric-cohomological-range** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-geometric-range)): Let $V$ be a smooth projective variety of dimension $n\ge2$, and let $\mathcal R$ be a locally finite slicing in which every point sheaf is stable of phase $1$. Then $$\begin{aligned} \mathcal R(0,1]&\subset D^{[-n+1,0]}(V), &\mathop{\mathrm{Coh}}(V)&\subset\mathcal R(1-n,1],\label{eq:geometric-range}\\ D^{\le-n}(V)&\subset\mathcal R(>0), &\mathcal R(>0)&\subset D^{\le0}(V).\label{eq:geometric-aisles} \end{aligned}$$ The sheaf $\mathcal H^{-n+1}(E)$ is torsion-free for $E\in\mathcal R(0,1]$. Moreover, $\mathcal H^0(E)$ is zero-dimensional for $E\in\mathcal R(1)$.
  - Explanation: Stable point sheaves constrain the cohomological amplitude of the geometric heart and the phase range of coherent sheaves. The bottom heart cohomology is torsion-free, while phase-one objects have zero-dimensional top cohomology.
- **claim-primitive-class-open-charge-stability** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-primitive-point)): Let a stability condition have support on a finite-rank lattice $\Lambda$. Suppose that an object $E$ has primitive class in $\Lambda$ and remains semistable throughout a neighborhood which is open in all central charges $\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(\Lambda,\mathbb C)$. Then $E$ is stable.
  - Explanation: A primitive-class object cannot remain strictly semistable throughout an open neighborhood in the full charge space. An independent perturbation of a stable factor's charge would otherwise destabilize it.
- **claim-uniform-ambient-charge-deformation** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-uniform-ambient-deformation)): For every $0<\epsilon<1/8$, there are open neighborhoods $V\ni B_*$ and $W\ni H_*$ in the real divisor and ample spaces, and $T\ge1$, such that for all $B\in V$, $H\in W$, and $t\ge T$, the charge <a href="#eq:deformed-ambient-charge" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:deformed-ambient-charge">[eq:deformed-ambient-charge]</a> lifts to a stability condition with slicing at distance less than $\epsilon$ from $\mathcal R_t$. The construction is available in an open neighborhood of that charge in the full space $\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(K_{\mathrm{num}}(Y),\mathbb C)$.
  - Explanation: The corrected ambient charges lift uniformly near the weighted ambient slicing throughout a sufficiently small real sector at large volume. Each lift also exists on an open neighborhood in the full numerical charge space.
- **claim-prescribed-geometric-restriction** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-geometric-prescribed-charges)): For either factor in <a href="#eq:two-character-factors" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:two-character-factors">[eq:two-character-factors]</a>, the choices in Lemma <a href="#lem:uniform-ambient-deformation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:uniform-ambient-deformation">9</a> can be made so that every $B\in V,H\in W,t\ge T$ gives a locally finite geometric stability condition on $X$, with charge <a href="#eq:exact-restricted-charge" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:exact-restricted-charge">[eq:exact-restricted-charge]</a> and full numerical support. Every point sheaf is stable of phase $1$. For $\Theta=1$, the construction also gives compatible geometric slicings on every smooth section $S_h\in\|L_h\|$, with Harder–Narasimhan phases computed by pushforward to $Y$.
  - Explanation: After verifying finite-map restriction hypotheses, ambient deformation restricts to locally finite geometric stability conditions with the exact prescribed charges and full numerical support. The ordinary construction also supplies compatible surface slicings.
- **claim-surface-geometric-single-tilt** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-surface-geometric-heart)): Let $S$ be a smooth projective surface and $(Z,\mathcal R)$ a locally finite geometric stability condition, normalized so that point sheaves have phase $1$. Suppose $$\Im Z(G)=\omega\bigl(c_1(G)-\beta\mathop{\mathrm{rk}}(G)\bigr),$$ where $\omega$ is a real ample divisor class and $\beta$ a real divisor class. Then $$\mathcal R(0,1]=\langle\mathcal F_{\omega,\beta}[1], \mathcal T_{\omega,\beta}\rangle,$$ where $\mathcal T_{\omega,\beta}$ has strictly positive slope factors (including torsion) and $\mathcal F_{\omega,\beta}$ has nonpositive slope factors.
  - Explanation: The specified ample-degree imaginary charge determines the surface geometric heart as the slope tilt. The nonpositive boundary belongs to the shifted torsion-free part.
- **claim-weighted-heart-cohomology-gap** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-heart-cohomology-slopes)): Every $E\in\mathcal C$ has cohomology in degrees $[-2,0]$, its sheaf $\mathcal H^{-2}(E)$ is torsion-free, and $$\label{eq:heart-sign-bounds} m^+_{H,B}(\mathcal H^{-2}(E))\le-c, \qquad m^-_{H,B}(\mathcal H^0(E))>c.$$ A bound for a zero sheaf is vacuous.
  - Explanation: Positive weighted surface restrictions impose opposite slope bounds on the outer cohomology sheaves of the constructed heart. These bounds separate them from the boundary of the ordinary first slope tilt.
- **claim-first-tilt-phase-comparison** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-first-tilt-phase-range)): We have $$\label{eq:first-tilt-phase-range} \mathcal B\subset\mathcal P(-1,1].$$ Consequently $$\mathcal T_0=\mathcal B\cap\mathcal P(0,1],\qquad \mathcal F_0=\mathcal B\cap\mathcal P(-1,0]$$ is a torsion pair in $\mathcal B$, and $\mathcal C=\langle\mathcal F_0[1],\mathcal T_0\rangle$.
  - Explanation: The ordinary first tilt lies in adjacent phase intervals of the constructed slicing. Intersecting it with the positive and nonpositive intervals gives a torsion pair whose tilt recovers the constructed heart.
- **claim-phase-one-first-tilt-points** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-phase-one-first-tilt)): An object of $\mathcal B\cap\mathcal P(1)$ is a zero-dimensional sheaf.
  - Explanation: The phase-one boundary inside the first tilt consists of zero-dimensional sheaves. The weighted surface slope comparison excludes a nonzero negative cohomology sheaf.
- **claim-physical-denominator-boundary** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-physical-zero-denominator)): For $E\in\mathcal B$ the denominator in <a href="#eq:physical-second-slope" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:physical-second-slope">[eq:physical-second-slope]</a> is nonnegative. If it vanishes, then $\Im Z_{B,tH}(E)\ge0$, with equality only for a zero-dimensional sheaf.
  - Explanation: The physical second slope has a nonnegative denominator on the first tilt. At denominator zero, the imaginary charge is nonnegative and vanishes only on zero-dimensional sheaves.
- **claim-exact-ordinary-double-tilt-heart** (classification, [source](#stmt-prop-exact-double-tilt-heart)): For the factor $\Theta=1$ throughout the real sector of Proposition <a href="#prop:geometric-prescribed-charges" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:geometric-prescribed-charges">10</a>, $$\mathcal P(0,1]=\mathcal A_{B,tH}.$$
  - Explanation: The ordinary prescribed-charge heart equals the standard double tilt throughout the constructed real sector. The proof identifies both torsion-pair parts, including the zero-slope boundary.
- **claim-tilt-denominator-and-subobject-criterion** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-tilt-zero-denominator)): An object $E\in\mathcal B_{H,B}$ has $d_B(E)\ge0$. Write $n=w_B-a^2r/2$. If $d_B(E)=0$, then $n(E)\ge0$, with equality exactly when $E$ is a zero-dimensional sheaf, including the zero object. For a finite-slope object $E$, semistability is equivalent to the condition that every proper nonzero subobject has positive denominator and slope at most $\nu(E)$. Every finite-slope quotient of a finite-slope semistable object has slope at least $\nu(E)$.
  - Explanation: Zero tilt denominator forces a nonnegative numerator, with simultaneous vanishing exactly for zero-dimensional sheaves. At finite slope, semistability can be checked against the object's slope once denominator-zero subobjects are excluded.
- **claim-reduced-tilt-character-support** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-tilt-support-bound)): For fixed $a>0$ there is a constant $C_a$, depending only on $a,X,H$, such that every tilt-semistable object, at any real twist $B$, satisfies $$\label{eq:tilt-support-bound} \|r\|+\|w_B\|+\\|c_B\\|\le C_a(d_B+\|n\|),\qquad n=w_B-a^2r/2.$$ The constants can be bounded uniformly when $a$ ranges in a compact subinterval of $(0,\infty)$.
  - Explanation: The reduced tilt charge controls rank, the full first character, and the polarized degree of the second character uniformly over real twists. This estimate does not control the full numerical Grothendieck group.
- **claim-finite-equal-slope-tilt-filtration** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-tilt-factors)): Every nonzero finite-slope tilt-semistable object has a finite filtration in $\mathcal B_{H,B}$ whose factors are tilt-stable of the same finite slope. Extensions of finite-slope semistable objects of the same slope are semistable of that slope.
  - Explanation: Finite-slope semistable objects admit finite stable-factor filtrations even at irrational twists. Equal-slope extensions remain semistable under the subobject–quotient convention.
- **claim-semistability-entire-open-semicircle** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-tilt-semicircle)): Suppose $E$ is tilt-semistable of finite slope $u$ at $(a,b)$. Put $c=b+u$ and $R=\sqrt{a^2+u^2}$. Then $E$ is tilt-semistable at every point $$\label{eq:tilt-semicircle} b'=c+x,\qquad a'=\sqrt{R^2-x^2},\qquad -R<x<R,$$ and its slope there is $-x$. In particular it is semistable of slope zero at $(R,c)$.
  - Explanation: A finite-slope semistable object remains semistable on its entire numerical open semicircle. The apex has slope zero, allowing inequalities proved at zero slope to be applied to arbitrary finite slopes.
- **claim-derived-duality-with-point-defect** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-tilt-duality)): Let $a>0$, let $B$ be real, and let $E\in\mathcal B_{H,B}$ be tilt-semistable of finite slope $u$. There are a zero-dimensional sheaf $T_0$ and a distinguished triangle $$\label{eq:tilt-dual-triangle} \widetilde E\longrightarrow\mathbb D(E)\longrightarrow T_0[-1] \longrightarrow\widetilde E[1],$$ where $\widetilde E\in\mathcal B_{H,-B}$ is tilt-semistable of slope $-u$. At twists $B$ and $-B$ respectively their coordinates are related by $$\label{eq:tilt-dual-characters} (r,d,w,z)(\widetilde E) =\bigl(-r(E),d_B(E),-w_B(E),z_B(E)+\operatorname{length}(T_0)/h\bigr).$$ Moreover $\widetilde E=\tau^{\le0}\mathbb D(E)$ for the ordinary cohomological truncation.
  - Explanation: The ordinary truncation of the shifted derived dual is tilt-semistable at the opposite twist with opposite slope. A zero-dimensional defect increases its third twisted character while reversing rank.
- **claim-restriction-slope-variance** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-restriction-variance)): Let $G$ be a slope-semistable torsion-free sheaf of rank $r>0$ on $X$. For a very general smooth $S\in\|H\|$, restrict $G$ and refine its slope Harder–Narasimhan filtration into slope-stable torsion-free factors $G_i$, with ranks $r_i$ and normalized slopes $\mu_i$. Then $$\label{eq:restriction-variance} \sum_i r_i\bigl(\mu_i-\mu(G)\bigr)^2\le r\xi_G.$$ The same assertion holds for a slope-semistable torsion-free sheaf on $S$, restricted to a very general smooth $C_m\in\|mH\|_S$, for every positive integer $m$.* *More generally, restrict a finite filtration with semistable torsion-free factors and refine each restricted factor separately. From $X$ to $S$, for any fixed divisor twist $T$ on $X$, restricted to $S$, the increases in $\sum_i r_i\mu_T(G_i)^2$ and in $\sum_i r_i\delta_{G_i}^T$ are equal and lie between zero and the original sum $\sum_G r(G)\xi_G$. From $S$ to $C_m$, the same upper bound holds for the increase of the second slope moment, using any fixed divisor twist on $S$ and its restriction to $C_m$. The concatenated filtration need not be the Harder–Narasimhan filtration of the whole restriction.
  - Explanation: Very general restrictions bound the rank-weighted slope variance by the original discriminant. Restricting a finite filtration factorwise controls the increase of numerical moments without asserting that the concatenation is the whole restriction's Harder–Narasimhan filtration.
- **claim-uniform-smooth-section-families** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-uniform-sections)): There is an integer $m>0$, depending only on $X,H$, such that every smooth $S\in\|H\|$ satisfies $$\label{eq:surface-fixed-m} h^0(S,\mathcal O_S(mH))=\chi(\mathcal O_S(mH)), \qquad \mu(K_S)-m\le-1,$$ and $H^0(X,\mathcal O_X(mH))\to H^0(S,\mathcal O_S(mH))$ is surjective. One can choose relatively compact analytic neighborhoods of a smooth transverse pair of divisors in $\|H\|\times\|mH\|$ such that the resulting surfaces and curves, equipped with the induced Fubini–Study metrics, have uniform smooth local geometry.* *Within these neighborhoods one may impose, for each application, the very-general restriction conditions of Lemma <a href="#lem:restriction-variance" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:restriction-variance">22</a> and exactness of any specified finite collection of restricted sequences. Restrictions of a specified finite collection of torsion-free sheaves may also be required to remain torsion-free. For a specified bounded complex, one may require its derived restriction to have ordinary cohomology equal to the restrictions of its ordinary cohomology sheaves.
  - Explanation: A geometry-dependent section degree and compact analytic neighborhoods provide uniform smooth geometry. Very-general and finite exactness conditions can subsequently be imposed for each collection of sheaves and complexes.
- **claim-rank-independent-connection-heat** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-uniform-heat)): Let $Y$ range over the compact families of curves or surfaces in Lemma <a href="#lem:uniform-sections" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:uniform-sections">23</a>, with real dimension $n$. Write $\Delta_{\mathrm{sc}}$ for the nonnegative scalar Laplacian. Its heat kernel satisfies $$0\le k_s(x,y)\le C s^{-n/2}\qquad(0<s\le1).$$ For any Hermitian vector bundle with metric connection $\nabla$, put $L=\nabla^*\nabla+1$. The kernel $K_s(x,y)$ of $e^{-sL}$ satisfies $$\label{eq:connection-heat} \\|K_s(x,y)\\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le C s^{-n/2} \qquad(s>0),$$ with a constant independent of the bundle, its rank, and its connection.
  - Explanation: Uniform scalar heat bounds on the chosen compact families dominate connection heat kernels in operator norm. The connection estimate is independent of the bundle rank and connection.
- **claim-subsheaf-evaluation-rank-sections** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-subsheaf-sections)): Let $Y$ be one of the curves or surfaces in Lemma <a href="#lem:uniform-sections" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:uniform-sections">23</a>, let $G$ be a slope-stable torsion-free sheaf on $Y$, and let $J\subset G$ have rank $q$. Then $$\label{eq:subsheaf-sections} h^0(Y,J)\le Cq\bigl(1+\mu(G)_+\bigr)^{\dim Y}, \qquad x_+=\max\{x,0\},$$ where $C$ depends only on the chosen geometric families. The same bound applies to subsheaves of a stable dual bundle tensored with a line bundle, using the slope of that twisted bundle.
  - Explanation: Sections of a subsheaf are controlled by its own rank and the positive slope of the stable ambient sheaf. The evaluation-rank argument avoids a factor proportional to the ambient rank.
- **claim-matrix-trace-spectral-dimension** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-matrix-spectral)): Let $S$ be one of the surfaces in Lemma <a href="#lem:uniform-sections" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:uniform-sections">23</a>. Let $\mathcal V$ be a Hermitian vector bundle with metric connection, $L=\nabla^*\nabla+1$, and let $P$ be a bounded measurable nonnegative selfadjoint endomorphism of $\mathcal V$. If a finite-dimensional subspace $U$ of the form domain of $L$ satisfies $$\label{eq:spectral-form-hypothesis} \langle Lu,u\rangle\le\langle Pu,u\rangle\qquad(u\in U),$$ then $$\label{eq:matrix-spectral-bound} \dim U\le C\int_S\operatorname{tr}(P^2).$$ The constant is independent of $\mathcal V$, its rank, $\nabla$, and $P$.
  - Explanation: A subspace satisfying the quadratic-form domination condition has dimension bounded by the integral of the squared matrix potential. Retaining the matrix trace keeps the constant independent of bundle rank.
- **claim-negative-surface-cohomology-error-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-negative-surface-cohomology)): Fix a real number $M$. There is a number $A_0$, depending only on $X,H,B_0,M$ and the chosen smooth neighborhoods, with the following property. For $A\ge A_0$ and a finite set $I\subset\{t\in\mathbb Z:t\le M\}$, there is a constant $C$ such that, for every chosen surface $S$, every $b\in[-1,1]$, every finite collection of slope-stable torsion-free sheaves $G_i$ on $S$, and every $t\in I$, $$\label{eq:negative-surface-cohomology} \sum_i h^1(S,G_i(tH)) \le C\sum_i r_i\left( \left\\|\frac{c_1(G_i)}{r_i}-T\right\\|^2+\xi_{G_i}\right), \qquad T=B_0\|_S+(b-A)H.$$ The constant is independent of the collection and its ranks. The same upper bound holds for $h^1(S,G(tH))$ if the $G_i$ are the factors of a filtration of $G$. In particular, a bound $C_0e$ for the sum on the right gives a bound $CC_0e$ for the first cohomology.
  - Explanation: Surface first cohomology is bounded by first-character deviation and discriminant energy near a sufficiently negative scalar class. The bound has no additional bare-rank term, and its threshold depends on the upper twist endpoint before the finite twist set is fixed.
- **claim-fixed-apex-discriminant-excess** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-apex)): Let $X$ be a smooth projective complex threefold, let $H$ be very ample, and let $B_0$ be a rational divisor class with $H^2B_0=0$. There are constants $A>0$ and $C\geq0$, depending only on $X,H,B_0$, such that, for every $b\in\mathbb R$ and every finite-slope $\nu_{A,b}$-semistable object $E\in\mathcal B_{H,B_0+bH}$ with $\nu_{A,b}(E)=0$, $$\label{eq:apex-estimate} z_B(E)-\frac{A^2}{6}d_B(E) \leq C\bigl(d_B(E)-A\|r(E)\|\bigr),\qquad B=B_0+bH.$$ The same constants can be chosen for $B_0$ and $-B_0$.
  - Explanation: At a fixed positive volume, the third-character defect is bounded by the denominator's excess over the absolute-rank term. The constants are uniform in the object and scalar twist and can be chosen for both transverse-twist signs.
- **claim-uniform-frobenius-presentation** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-frobenius-presentation)): There are positive integers $j,j_2$ and a constant $C_0$, determined by $X,H,D$, with the following property. After choosing and shrinking a model of this fixed geometry over a finitely generated integral subring of $\mathbb C$, every smooth geometric fiber in characteristic $p$ and every $\|b\|\leq1$ satisfy the following assertion, for either $L=L_p$ or $L=L'_p$: $$G_{p,L}=F_*(K_X^{1-p}\otimes L^{-1})$$ has a presentation $$\label{eq:frobenius-presentation} 0\longrightarrow K_{p,L}\longrightarrow\mathcal O_X(-j)^{N_{p,L}} \longrightarrow G_{p,L}\longrightarrow0, \qquad N_{p,L}\leq C_0p^3,$$ where $K_{p,L}(j_2)$ is generated by its global sections.
  - Explanation: Fixed geometric regularity data give presentations of the relevant Frobenius pushforwards on smooth fibers. The generator count has cubic characteristic growth and the kernel is globally generated after a fixed twist.
- **claim-frobenius-sections-fixed-twist-transfer** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-frobenius-section-transfer)): On a smooth geometric fiber in the preceding lemma, let $P_1$ be a perfect complex with ordinary cohomology in degrees $[-1,0]$. If $$H^0\bigl(\mathcal H^{-1}(P_1)(j_2)\bigr)=0,$$ then, for either $L=L_p$ or $L=L'_p$, $$\label{eq:frobenius-section-transfer} h^0(F^*P_1\otimes L) \leq C_0p^3h^0(P_1(j)).$$ The twists $j,j_2$ and the constant $C_0$ are those chosen from the fixed geometry in Lemma <a href="#lem:frobenius-presentation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:frobenius-presentation">29</a>.
  - Explanation: A fixed negative-cohomology vanishing makes the presentation-induced Hom map injective. Frobenius sections are consequently bounded by the original complex's sections at a fixed twist times the presentation bound.
- **claim-residual-restriction-image-dimension** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-restriction-image)): Let $E\in\mathcal B_{H,B_0+bH}$ be finite-slope $\nu_{A,b}$-semistable with $\nu_{A,b}(E)=0$ and $\|b\|\leq1$. Put $B=B_0+bH$ and $e=d_B(E)-A\|r(E)\|$. Let $P$ be the residual term in <a href="#eq:positive-residual-triangle" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:positive-residual-triangle">[eq:positive-residual-triangle]</a> when $r(E)\geq0$, or in <a href="#eq:negative-residual-triangle" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:negative-residual-triangle">[eq:negative-residual-triangle]</a> when $r(E)\leq0$. Let $W\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathcal O_X(-tH),P)$ be any finite-dimensional subspace, where $t_0<t\leq M$ is an integer. There is a very general smooth $S\in\|H\|$ in the fixed neighborhood, depending on this finite data, such that the image of $$W\longrightarrow\mathbb H^0\bigl(S,P\|_S(t)\bigr)$$ has dimension at most $C_2e$. The constant is independent of $W$ and its dimension.
  - Explanation: For each finite section subspace, a suitable very general surface bounds the dimension of its residual restriction image by the discriminant excess. Cancellation against the evaluation kernel removes dependence on the source dimension.
- **claim-residual-fixed-twist-sections** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-residual-sections)): Let $E\in\mathcal B_{H,B_0+bH}$ be finite-slope $\nu_{A,b}$-semistable with $\nu_{A,b}(E)=0$ and $\|b\|\leq1$. Put $B=B_0+bH$ and $e=d_B(E)-A\|r(E)\|$. For $r(E)\geq0$, take $P$ from <a href="#eq:positive-residual-triangle" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:positive-residual-triangle">[eq:positive-residual-triangle]</a> and put $V_P=\mathcal H^{-1}(P)$. For $r(E)\leq0$, use the decomposition <a href="#eq:negative-residual-triangle" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:negative-residual-triangle">[eq:negative-residual-triangle]</a> and put $V=\mathcal H^{-1}(E)$. There is a constant $C_3$, independent of $E,b$, such that $$\label{eq:residual-sections} \begin{aligned} h^0(P(j))&\leq C_3e &&\text{for the positive decomposition},\\ h^0(E(j))&\leq C_3e &&\text{for the negative decomposition}. \end{aligned}$$ Moreover $$\label{eq:negative-cohomology-vanishing} \begin{aligned} H^0(V_P(j_2))&=0&&\text{in the positive decomposition},\\ H^0(V(j_2))&=0&&\text{in the negative decomposition}. \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: Telescoping restriction images bounds sections of the positive residual or the nonpositive-rank object by the same excess. The required negative-cohomology sheaf sections vanish at the presentation's fixed twist.
- **claim-fixed-sheaf-semistable-spreading** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-fixed-sheaf-spreading)): Let $G$ be a slope-semistable torsion-free sheaf on a smooth projective complex variety $Y$ with a fixed very ample class. There is a model and a localization on which the geometric fibers of $G$ are torsion-free and slope-semistable. Finitely many such requirements may be imposed simultaneously.
  - Explanation: A fixed slope-semistable torsion-free complex sheaf admits a localized model whose geometric fibers retain these properties. Finitely many fixed sheaf requirements can be enforced simultaneously.
- **claim-frobenius-maximal-slope-gap** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-frobenius-slope-gap)): On a smooth projective geometric fiber in characteristic $p$, let $G$ be a slope-semistable torsion-free sheaf of rank $r$. For a constant $c_Y$ fixed by the chosen model of $Y$ and its polarization, $$\label{eq:frobenius-slope-gap} \mu_{\max}(F^*G)\leq p\mu(G)+c_Y(r-1).$$
  - Explanation: The canonical connection and Cartier descent bound the maximal slope of a Frobenius pullback by its scaled original slope plus a rank-dependent gap term. The cotangent embedding fixes the geometric gap constant.
- **claim-apex-to-global-wall-transport** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-apex-transport)): Fix a smooth projective complex threefold $X$, an integral ample class $H$, and $B_0\in N^1(X)_{\mathbb Q}$. Suppose that there are $A>0$ and $C\ge0$ such that every finite-slope tilt-semistable object of slope zero at $(A,b)$, for every $b\in\mathbb R$, satisfies $$\label{eq:wall-apex-input} D_A(E)\le C S_A(E).$$ Then, with $$\label{eq:wall-constants} R_0=A+\frac{3C}{A},\qquad k_0=\max\left\{C+\frac{A^2}{6},\frac{CR_0}{A}\right\},$$ every finite-slope tilt-semistable object of slope zero at $(a,b)$ satisfies $D_a(E)\le k_0d_b(E)$ for all $a>0,b\in\mathbb R$, and $D_a(E)\le0$ whenever $a>R_0$.
  - Explanation: A uniform fixed-apex defect estimate yields an explicit all-volume correction and an uncorrected tail. Differential comparison preserves violations along zero-slope branches, while rationality makes discriminant descent terminate at genuine walls.
- **claim-reduced-exceptional-surface-uniform-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-exceptional-surface-bound)): Let $X$ be a smooth projective complex threefold, let $H$ be an ample integral divisor, and let $i:D\hookrightarrow X$ be a smooth integral divisor such that $H\|_D=-D\|_D$. Fix $B_0\in N^1(X)_{\mathbb Q}$, and set $B=B_0+bH$. If $F$ is a torsion-free sheaf of positive rank on $D$ and $i_*F$ is $\nu_{a,b}$-semistable of slope zero, then $$\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_3^B(i_*F)}{H^2\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_1^B(i_*F)}\le\frac1{24}. \label{eq:exceptional-uniform}$$ Consequently these objects satisfy the corrected strong inequality with $\Gamma=H^2/24$ for every $a>0$, and the uncorrected strong inequality for every $a\ge1/2$. Both constants are independent of $F$, its rank, and $b$.
  - Explanation: A torsion-free sheaf on the specified smooth reduced divisor has a uniformly bounded third-character ratio when its pushforward is tilt-semistable of slope zero. The corrected and uncorrected bounds are independent of its rank and scalar twist.

        "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be a smooth projective complex threefold, $H$ an ample integral divisor, and $B_0\\in N^1(X)_{\\mathbb Q}$. There are constants $k\\in\\mathbb Q_{\\geq0}$ and $R_*>0$, depending only on $(X,H,B_0)$, such that every finite-slope $\\nu_{a,b}$-semistable $E\\in\\mathcal B_{H,B_0+bH}$ with $\\nu_{a,b}(E)=0$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:intro-corrected}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{ch}}_3^{B_0+bH}(E)\n \\leq \\left(\\frac{a^2}{6}+k\\right)H^2\\mathop{\\mathrm{ch}}_1^{B_0+bH}(E)\n \\qquad(a>0,\\ b\\in\\mathbb R).$$ For the same fixed data, the stronger bound $$\\label{eq:intro-tail}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{ch}}_3^{B_0+bH}(E)\n \\leq \\frac{a^2}{6}H^2\\mathop{\\mathrm{ch}}_1^{B_0+bH}(E)$$ holds whenever $a>R_*$, for every $b\\in\\mathbb R$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-full-numerical-embedding",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be a smooth projective threefold. Rational Chern characters identify its numerical Grothendieck group with $$\\label{eq:full-numerical-lattice}\n K_{\\mathrm{num}}(X)_\\mathbb Q\\simeq\n \\mathbb Q\\oplus N^1(X)_\\mathbb Q\\oplus N_1(X)_\\mathbb Q\\oplus\\mathbb Q.$$ Products of numerical divisor classes span this space. Given a real ample class $H_*$, there are very ample line bundles $L_1,\\ldots,L_q$ whose classes form a basis of $N^1(X)_\\mathbb R$ and positive numbers $s_1,\\ldots,s_q$ with $H_*=\\sum_hs_hL_h$. For the product embedding $$\\label{eq:product-embedding}\n i:X\\hookrightarrow Y=\\prod_{h=1}^q\\mathbb P^{d_h},\n \\qquad d_h=h^0(X,L_h)-1,$$ the pushforward $i_*:K_{\\mathrm{num}}(X)_\\mathbb R\\to K_{\\mathrm{num}}(Y)_\\mathbb R$ is injective.",
        "source_id": "PM-055.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

For the product embedding $$\\label{eq:product-embedding}\n i:X\\hookrightarrow Y=\\prod_{h=1}^q\\mathbb P^{d_h},\n \\qquad d_h=h^0(X,L_h)-1,$$ the pushforward $i_*:K_{\\mathrm{num}}(X)_\\mathbb R\\to K_{\\mathrm{num}}(Y)_\\mathbb R$ is injective.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-elliptic-scaled-support",
        "exact_quote": "Let $C$ be an elliptic curve, let $n\\ge1$, and fix positive rational numbers $\\rho_1,\\ldots,\\rho_n$. For rational $R\\ge1$, put $w_r=R\\rho_r$ and let $H_r$ be the point-divisor class from the $r$th factor of $C^n$. The product stability condition with charge $$Z_{\\mathbf w}(u)=-\\int_{C^n}e^{-i\\sum_rw_rH_r}\\mathop{\\mathrm{ch}}(u)$$ has support on the coordinate lattice $$x_I(u)=H_I\\mathop{\\mathrm{ch}}_{n-|I|}(u),\\qquad\n H_I=\\prod_{r\\in I}H_r,\\qquad I\\subset\\{1,\\ldots,n\\}.$$ More precisely, set $y_I=(\\prod_{r\\in I}w_r)x_I$. In these coordinates, $$Z_{\\mathbf w}(u)=\\widehat Z_n(y(u)),\\qquad\n \\widehat Z_n(y)=-\\sum_I(-i)^{|I|}y_I.$$ For a fixed Euclidean norm there is $C_n>0$, independent of $R$, such that $$\\|y(u)\\|\\le C_n|Z_{\\mathbf w}(u)|\n \\qquad(u\\text{ the class of a semistable object}).$$ The constants can be chosen uniformly when the positive ratios $\\rho_r$ vary in a compact set. Point sheaves are stable of phase $1$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-weighted-ambient",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Y=\\prod_{h=1}^q\\mathbb P^{d_h}$, put $\\xi_h=c_1(\\mathcal O_h(1))$, and fix $s_h>0$. There is a continuous family of numerical stability conditions $(U_R,\\mathcal R_R)$ for $R\\ge1$ with $$U_R(u)=-\\int_Ye^{-iR\\sum_hs_h\\xi_h}\\mathop{\\mathrm{ch}}(u).$$ All point sheaves are stable of phase $1$. Write $\\mathop{\\mathrm{ch}}(u)=\\sum_{\\mathbf j}u_{\\mathbf j}\\xi^{\\mathbf j}$, where $0\\le j_h\\le d_h$, and set $$\\label{eq:ambient-scaled-coordinates}\n (M_Ru)_{\\mathbf j}=\n u_{\\mathbf j}\\prod_h(Rs_h)^{d_h-j_h}.$$ For a fixed Euclidean norm there is $D>0$, independent of $R$, such that $$\\label{eq:ambient-support}\n \\|M_Ru\\|\\le D|U_R(u)|\\qquad\n (u\\text{ the class of an }\\mathcal R_R\\text{-semistable object}).$$ For every $\\mathbf e\\in\\mathbb Z^q$, $$\\label{eq:ambient-twist-distance}\n d(\\mathcal R_R,\\mathcal R_R\\otimes\\mathcal O_Y(\\mathbf e))\n \\le\\sum_h\\frac{d_h|e_h|}{\\pi Rs_h}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-055.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-weighted-ambient",
          "exact_quote": "Let $Y=\\prod_{h=1}^q\\mathbb P^{d_h}$, put $\\xi_h=c_1(\\mathcal O_h(1))$, and fix $s_h>0$. There is a continuous family of numerical stability conditions $(U_R,\\mathcal R_R)$ for $R\\ge1$ with $$U_R(u)=-\\int_Ye^{-iR\\sum_hs_h\\xi_h}\\mathop{\\mathrm{ch}}(u).$$ All point sheaves are stable of phase $1$. Write $\\mathop{\\mathrm{ch}}(u)=\\sum_{\\mathbf j}u_{\\mathbf j}\\xi^{\\mathbf j}$, where $0\\le j_h\\le d_h$, and set $$\\label{eq:ambient-scaled-coordinates}\n (M_Ru)_{\\mathbf j}=\n u_{\\mathbf j}\\prod_h(Rs_h)^{d_h-j_h}.$$ For a fixed Euclidean norm there is $D>0$, independent of $R$, such that $$\\label{eq:ambient-support}\n \\|M_Ru\\|\\le D|U_R(u)|\\qquad\n (u\\text{ the class of an }\\mathcal R_R\\text{-semistable object}).$$ For every $\\mathbf e\\in\\mathbb Z^q$, $$\\label{eq:ambient-twist-distance}\n d(\\mathcal R_R,\\mathcal R_R\\otimes\\mathcal O_Y(\\mathbf e))\n \\le\\sum_h\\frac{d_h|e_h|}{\\pi Rs_h}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-055.2"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-geometric-range",
        "exact_quote": "Let $V$ be a smooth projective variety of dimension $n\\ge2$, and let $\\mathcal R$ be a locally finite slicing in which every point sheaf is stable of phase $1$.       },

      "explanation": "After verifying finite-map restriction hypotheses, ambient deformation restricts to locally finite geometric stability conditions with the exact prescribed charges and full numerical support. The ordinary construction also supplies compatible surface slicings.",

        {
          "anchor": "eq:two-character-factors",
          "exact_quote": "Fix real classes $B_*,H_*$ with $H_*$ ample, and choose the embedding in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:full-numerical-embedding\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:full-numerical-embedding\">4</a>. We write $L_h$ also for its first Chern class, so $i^*\\xi_h=L_h$ and $H_*=\\sum_hs_hL_h$ with $s_h>0$. Consider separately the factors $$\\label{eq:two-character-factors}\n \\Theta=1\\qquad\\text{and}\\qquad\n \\Theta=\\sqrt{\\mathop{\\mathrm{td}}(X)}=1+\\frac{c_2(X)}{24}.$$ The assumption on the canonical bundle gives $\\mathop{\\mathrm{td}}(X)=1+c_2(X)/12$ in the degrees relevant on $X$. By Lemma <a href=\"#lem:full-numerical-embedding\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:full-numerical-embedding\">4</a>, choose a degree-two polynomial $\\delta$ in the $\\xi_h$ with $i^*\\delta=c_2(X)$ numerically. A suitable polynomial on $Y$ with constant term one is $$\\gamma=\\begin{cases}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{td}}(Y)(1-\\delta/12),&\\Theta=1,\\\\\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{td}}(Y)(1-\\delta/24),&\\Theta=\\sqrt{\\mathop{\\mathrm{td}}(X)}.\n \\end{cases}$$ Products are taken in the finite-dimensional numerical Chow ring. Thus $$\\label{eq:gamma-lift}\n i^*\\bigl(\\gamma/\\mathop{\\mathrm{td}}(Y)\\bigr)\\mathop{\\mathrm{td}}(X)=\\Theta.$$ In particular the two choices remove different Todd contributions.",

        }

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-surface-geometric-heart",
        "exact_quote": "Let $S$ be a smooth projective surface and $(Z,\\mathcal R)$ a locally finite geometric stability condition, normalized so that point sheaves have phase $1$. Suppose $$\\Im Z(G)=\\omega\\bigl(c_1(G)-\\beta\\mathop{\\mathrm{rk}}(G)\\bigr),$$ where $\\omega$ is a real ample divisor class and $\\beta$ a real divisor class. Then $$\\mathcal R(0,1]=\\langle\\mathcal F_{\\omega,\\beta}[1],\n                              \\mathcal T_{\\omega,\\beta}\\rangle,$$ where $\\mathcal T_{\\omega,\\beta}$ has strictly positive slope factors (including torsion) and $\\mathcal F_{\\omega,\\beta}$ has nonpositive slope factors.",
        "source_id": "PM-055.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-tilt-zero-denominator",
        "exact_quote": "An object $E\\in\\mathcal B_{H,B}$ has $d_B(E)\\ge0$. Write $n=w_B-a^2r/2$. If $d_B(E)=0$, then $n(E)\\ge0$, with equality exactly when $E$ is a zero-dimensional sheaf, including the zero object. For a finite-slope object $E$, semistability is equivalent to the condition that every proper nonzero subobject has positive denominator and slope at most $\\nu(E)$. Every finite-slope quotient of a finite-slope semistable object has slope at least $\\nu(E)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-055.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-tilt-support-bound",
        "exact_quote": "For fixed $a>0$ there is a constant $C_a$, depending only on $a,X,H$, such that every tilt-semistable object, at any real twist $B$, satisfies $$\\label{eq:tilt-support-bound}\n |r|+|w_B|+\\|c_B\\|\\le C_a(d_B+|n|),\\qquad n=w_B-a^2r/2.$$ The constants can be bounded uniformly when $a$ ranges in a compact subinterval of $(0,\\infty)$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-restriction-variance",
        "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be a slope-semistable torsion-free sheaf of rank $r>0$ on $X$. For a very general smooth $S\\in|H|$, restrict $G$ and refine its slope Harder–Narasimhan filtration into slope-stable torsion-free factors $G_i$, with ranks $r_i$ and normalized slopes $\\mu_i$. Then $$\\label{eq:restriction-variance}\n \\sum_i r_i\\bigl(\\mu_i-\\mu(G)\\bigr)^2\\le r\\xi_G.$$ The same assertion holds for a slope-semistable torsion-free sheaf on $S$, restricted to a very general smooth $C_m\\in|mH|_S$, for every positive integer $m$.*\n\n*More generally, restrict a finite filtration with semistable torsion-free factors and refine each restricted factor separately. From $X$ to $S$, for any fixed divisor twist $T$ on $X$, restricted to $S$, the increases in $\\sum_i r_i\\mu_T(G_i)^2$ and in $\\sum_i r_i\\delta_{G_i}^T$ are equal and lie between zero and the original sum $\\sum_G r(G)\\xi_G$. From $S$ to $C_m$, the same upper bound holds for the increase of the second slope moment, using any fixed divisor twist on $S$ and its restriction to $C_m$. The concatenated filtration need not be the Harder–Narasimhan filtration of the whole restriction.",
        "source_id": "PM-055.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-restriction-variance",
          "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be a slope-semistable torsion-free sheaf of rank $r>0$ on $X$. Then $$\\label{eq:restriction-variance}\n \\sum_i r_i\\bigl(\\mu_i-\\mu(G)\\bigr)^2\\le r\\xi_G.$$ The same assertion holds for a slope-semistable torsion-free sheaf on $S$, restricted to a very general smooth $C_m\\in|mH|_S$, for every positive integer $m$.*\n\n*More generally, restrict a finite filtration with semistable torsion-free factors and refine each restricted factor separately. From $X$ to $S$, for any fixed divisor twist $T$ on $X$, restricted to $S$, the increases in $\\sum_i r_i\\mu_T(G_i)^2$ and in $\\sum_i r_i\\delta_{G_i}^T$ are equal and lie between zero and the original sum $\\sum_G r(G)\\xi_G$. From $S$ to $C_m$, the same upper bound holds for the increase of the second slope moment, using any fixed divisor twist on $S$ and its restriction to $C_m$. The concatenated filtration need not be the Harder–Narasimhan filtration of the whole restriction.",
          "source_id": "PM-055.2"

      "exact_statement": "Let $G$ be a slope-semistable torsion-free sheaf of rank $r>0$ on $X$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-uniform-sections",
        "exact_quote": "There is an integer $m>0$, depending only on $X,H$, such that every smooth $S\\in|H|$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:surface-fixed-m}\n h^0(S,\\mathcal O_S(mH))=\\chi(\\mathcal O_S(mH)),\n \\qquad \\mu(K_S)-m\\le-1,$$ and $H^0(X,\\mathcal O_X(mH))\\to H^0(S,\\mathcal O_S(mH))$ is surjective. One can choose relatively compact analytic neighborhoods of a smooth transverse pair of divisors in $|H|\\times|mH|$ such that the resulting surfaces and curves, equipped with the induced Fubini–Study metrics, have uniform smooth local geometry.*\n\n*Within these neighborhoods one may impose, for each application, the very-general restriction conditions of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:restriction-variance\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:restriction-variance\">22</a> and exactness of any specified finite collection of restricted sequences. Restrictions of a specified finite collection of torsion-free sheaves may also be required to remain torsion-free. For a specified bounded complex, one may require its derived restriction to have ordinary cohomology equal to the restrictions of its ordinary cohomology sheaves.",
        "source_id": "PM-055.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-uniform-heat",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Y$ range over the compact families of curves or surfaces in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:uniform-sections\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:uniform-sections\">23</a>, with real dimension $n$. Write $\\Delta_{\\mathrm{sc}}$ for the nonnegative scalar Laplacian. Its heat kernel satisfies $$0\\le k_s(x,y)\\le C s^{-n/2}\\qquad(0<s\\le1).$$ For any Hermitian vector bundle with metric connection $\\nabla$, put $L=\\nabla^*\\nabla+1$. The kernel $K_s(x,y)$ of $e^{-sL}$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:connection-heat}\n \\|K_s(x,y)\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}\\le C s^{-n/2}\n \\qquad(s>0),$$ with a constant independent of the bundle, its rank, and its connection.",
        "source_id": "PM-055.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-subsheaf-sections",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Y$ be one of the curves or surfaces in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:uniform-sections\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:uniform-sections\">23</a>, let $G$ be a slope-stable torsion-free sheaf on $Y$, and let $J\\subset G$ have rank $q$. Then $$\\label{eq:subsheaf-sections}\n h^0(Y,J)\\le Cq\\bigl(1+\\mu(G)_+\\bigr)^{\\dim Y},\n \\qquad x_+=\\max\\{x,0\\},$$ where $C$ depends only on the chosen geometric families. The same bound applies to subsheaves of a stable dual bundle tensored with a line bundle, using the slope of that twisted bundle.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-matrix-spectral",
        "exact_quote": "Let $S$ be one of the surfaces in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:uniform-sections\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:uniform-sections\">23</a>. Let $\\mathcal V$ be a Hermitian vector bundle with metric connection, $L=\\nabla^*\\nabla+1$, and let $P$ be a bounded measurable nonnegative selfadjoint endomorphism of $\\mathcal V$. If a finite-dimensional subspace $U$ of the form domain of $L$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:spectral-form-hypothesis}\n \\langle Lu,u\\rangle\\le\\langle Pu,u\\rangle\\qquad(u\\in U),$$ then $$\\label{eq:matrix-spectral-bound}\n \\dim U\\le C\\int_S\\operatorname{tr}(P^2).$$ The constant is independent of $\\mathcal V$, its rank, $\\nabla$, and $P$.",

      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-negative-surface-cohomology",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a real number $M$. There is a number $A_0$, depending only on $X,H,B_0,M$ and the chosen smooth neighborhoods, with the following property. For $A\\ge A_0$ and a finite set $I\\subset\\{t\\in\\mathbb Z:t\\le M\\}$, there is a constant $C$ such that, for every chosen surface $S$, every $b\\in[-1,1]$, every finite collection of slope-stable torsion-free sheaves $G_i$ on $S$, and every $t\\in I$, $$\\label{eq:negative-surface-cohomology}\n \\sum_i h^1(S,G_i(tH))\n \\le C\\sum_i r_i\\left(\n  \\left\\|\\frac{c_1(G_i)}{r_i}-T\\right\\|^2+\\xi_{G_i}\\right),\n \\qquad T=B_0|_S+(b-A)H.$$ The constant is independent of the collection and its ranks. The same upper bound holds for $h^1(S,G(tH))$ if the $G_i$ are the factors of a filtration of $G$. In particular, a bound $C_0e$ for the sum on the right gives a bound $CC_0e$ for the first cohomology.",
        "source_id": "PM-055.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-negative-surface-cohomology",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a real number $M$. There is a number $A_0$, depending only on $X,H,B_0,M$ and the chosen smooth neighborhoods, with the following property. For $A\\ge A_0$ and a finite set $I\\subset\\{t\\in\\mathbb Z:t\\le M\\}$, there is a constant $C$ such that, for every chosen surface $S$, every $b\\in[-1,1]$, every finite collection of slope-stable torsion-free sheaves $G_i$ on $S$, and every $t\\in I$, $$\\label{eq:negative-surface-cohomology}\n \\sum_i h^1(S,G_i(tH))\n \\le C\\sum_i r_i\\left(\n  \\left\\|\\frac{c_1(G_i)}{r_i}-T\\right\\|^2+\\xi_{G_i}\\right),\n \\qquad T=B_0|_S+(b-A)H.$$ The constant is independent of the collection and its ranks. The same upper bound holds for $h^1(S,G(tH))$ if the $G_i$ are the factors of a filtration of $G$. In particular, a bound $C_0e$ for the sum on the right gives a bound $CC_0e$ for the first cohomology.",
          "source_id": "PM-055.2"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Fix a real number $M$. There is a number $A_0$, depending only on $X,H,B_0,M$ and the chosen smooth neighborhoods, with the following property.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-apex",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be a smooth projective complex threefold, let $H$ be very ample, and let $B_0$ be a rational divisor class with $H^2B_0=0$. There are constants $A>0$ and $C\\geq0$, depending only on $X,H,B_0$, such that, for every $b\\in\\mathbb R$ and every finite-slope $\\nu_{A,b}$-semistable object $E\\in\\mathcal B_{H,B_0+bH}$ with $\\nu_{A,b}(E)=0$, $$\\label{eq:apex-estimate}\n z_B(E)-\\frac{A^2}{6}d_B(E)\n \\leq C\\bigl(d_B(E)-A|r(E)|\\bigr),\\qquad B=B_0+bH.$$ The same constants can be chosen for $B_0$ and $-B_0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-055.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-apex",
          "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be a smooth projective complex threefold, let $H$ be very ample, and let $B_0$ be a rational divisor class with $H^2B_0=0$. There are constants $A>0$ and $C\\geq0$, depending only on $X,H,B_0$, such that, for every $b\\in\\mathbb R$ and every finite-slope $\\nu_{A,b}$-semistable object $E\\in\\mathcal B_{H,B_0+bH}$ with $\\nu_{A,b}(E)=0$, $$\\label{eq:apex-estimate}\n z_B(E)-\\frac{A^2}{6}d_B(E)\n \\leq C\\bigl(d_B(E)-A|r(E)|\\bigr),\\qquad B=B_0+bH.$$ The same constants can be chosen for $B_0$ and $-B_0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-055.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $X$ be a smooth projective complex threefold, let $H$ be very ample, and let $B_0$ be a rational divisor class with $H^2B_0=0$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-frobenius-presentation",
        "exact_quote": "There are positive integers $j,j_2$ and a constant $C_0$, determined by $X,H,D$, with the following property. After choosing and shrinking a model of this fixed geometry over a finitely generated integral subring of $\\mathbb C$, every smooth geometric fiber in characteristic $p$ and every $|b|\\leq1$ satisfy the following assertion, for either $L=L_p$ or $L=L'_p$: $$G_{p,L}=F_*(K_X^{1-p}\\otimes L^{-1})$$ has a presentation $$\\label{eq:frobenius-presentation}\n 0\\longrightarrow K_{p,L}\\longrightarrow\\mathcal O_X(-j)^{N_{p,L}}\n \\longrightarrow G_{p,L}\\longrightarrow0,\n \\qquad N_{p,L}\\leq C_0p^3,$$ where $K_{p,L}(j_2)$ is generated by its global sections.",
        "source_id": "PM-055.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-frobenius-presentation",
          "exact_quote": "There are positive integers $j,j_2$ and a constant $C_0$, determined by $X,H,D$, with the following property.           "source_id": "PM-055.2"
        }

        "exact_quote": "On a smooth geometric fiber in the preceding lemma, let $P_1$ be a perfect complex with ordinary cohomology in degrees $[-1,0]$.         "exact_quote": "Let $E\\in\\mathcal B_{H,B_0+bH}$ be finite-slope $\\nu_{A,b}$-semistable with $\\nu_{A,b}(E)=0$ and $|b|\\leq1$. Put $B=B_0+bH$ and $e=d_B(E)-A|r(E)|$.         "anchor": "stmt-prop-apex-transport",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a smooth projective complex threefold $X$, an integral ample class $H$, and $B_0\\in N^1(X)_{\\mathbb Q}$. Suppose that there are $A>0$ and $C\\ge0$ such that every finite-slope tilt-semistable object of slope zero at $(A,b)$, for every $b\\in\\mathbb R$, satisfies $$\\label{eq:wall-apex-input}\n D_A(E)\\le C S_A(E).$$ Then, with $$\\label{eq:wall-constants}\n R_0=A+\\frac{3C}{A},\\qquad\n k_0=\\max\\left\\{C+\\frac{A^2}{6},\\frac{CR_0}{A}\\right\\},$$ every finite-slope tilt-semistable object of slope zero at $(a,b)$ satisfies $D_a(E)\\le k_0d_b(E)$ for all $a>0,b\\in\\mathbb R$, and $D_a(E)\\le0$ whenever $a>R_0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-055.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-apex-transport",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a smooth projective complex threefold $X$, an integral ample class $H$, and $B_0\\in N^1(X)_{\\mathbb Q}$.           "source_id": "PM-055.2"
        }

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Numerical Grothendieck group** ([source](#pm-h-3)): The Euler pairing is $\chi(E,F)=\sum_j(-1)^j\dim\mathop{\mathrm{Ext}}^j(E,F)$, and we use $$K_{\mathrm{num}}(X)=K_0(X)/\{v:\chi(v,w)=0\text{ for every }w\in K_0(X)\}.$$
- **Numerical stability condition** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A numerical stability condition consists of a charge homomorphism $Z:K_{\mathrm{num}}(X)\to\mathbb C$ and a locally finite slicing $\mathcal P$ of $D^{\mathrm b}(X)$.
- **Geometric stability condition** ([source](#pm-h-3)): We call a stability condition geometric if all point sheaves $\mathcal O_x$ are stable of phase $1$.
- **Full numerical support** ([source](#sec:ambient)): A stability condition has *full numerical support* if, for a norm on $K_{\mathrm{num}}(X)_\mathbb R$, there is a constant $C$ such that $\\|[E]\\|\le C\|Z(E)\|$ for every semistable object $E$.
- **Ordinary physical central charge** ([source](#eq:intro-physical)): For real $B$, real ample $H$ and $t>0$, the ordinary physical charge is $$\label{eq:intro-physical} Z_{B,tH}(E)= -\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_3^B(E)+\frac{t^2}{2}H^2\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_1^B(E) +i\left(tH\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_2^B(E)-\frac{t^3}{6}H^3\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_0(E)\right).$$
- **Standard double-tilt heart** ([source](#eq:intro-physical)): The standard double-tilt heart is $$\mathcal A_{B,tH}= \langle\mathcal F'_{B,tH}[1],\mathcal T'_{B,tH}\rangle.$$
- **Slicing distance** ([source](#pm-h-10)): We use the distance $$d(\mathcal R,\mathcal R')= \sup_{E\ne0}\max\{ \|\phi^+_{\mathcal R}(E)-\phi^+_{\mathcal R'}(E)\|, \|\phi^-_{\mathcal R}(E)-\phi^-_{\mathcal R'}(E)\|\}.$$
- **Bayer property** ([source](#pm-h-10)): For a line bundle $L$ and an integer $k$, we say that $\mathcal R$ has the *Bayer property* for $(L,k)$ if $F\otimes L[k]$ has all phases at least $\phi$ whenever $F\in\mathcal R(\phi)$.
- **Normalized numerical characters** ([source](#pm-h-19)): For a real divisor class $T$, set $\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}^T(E)=e^{-T}\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}(E)$ and use the normalized coordinates $$\label{eq:normalized-characters} \begin{gathered} r(E)=\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_0(E),\qquad c_T(E)=\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_1^T(E),\\ d_T(E)=\frac{H^2c_T(E)}h,\qquad w_T(E)=\frac{H\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_2^T(E)}h,\qquad z_T(E)=\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_3^T(E)}h. \end{gathered}$$
- **First slope tilt** ([source](#pm-h-19)): The torsion pair and its tilt are $$\label{eq:first-tilt} \begin{gathered} \mathcal T_{H,B}=\{U:\mu^-_{H,B}(U)>0\},\qquad \mathcal F_{H,B}=\{V:\mu^+_{H,B}(V)\le0\},\\ \mathcal B_{H,B}=\langle\mathcal F_{H,B}[1],\mathcal T_{H,B}\rangle, \end{gathered}$$ where the zero sheaf belongs to both parts.
- **Tilt numerator and slope** ([source](#pm-h-19)): For $a>0$ put $$\label{eq:tilt-numerator} n_{a,b}=w_b-\tfrac12a^2r,\qquad \nu_{a,b}(E)= \begin{cases}n_{a,b}(E)/d_b(E),&d_b(E)>0,\\ +\infty,&d_b(E)=0. \end{cases}$$
- **Divisor Hodge norm** ([source](#eq:divisor-norm)): Define $$\label{eq:divisor-norm} \langle C,D\rangle=\frac{HCD}{h},\qquad C_\perp=C-\langle H,C\rangle H,\qquad \\|C\\|^2=\langle H,C\rangle^2-\langle C_\perp,C_\perp\rangle.$$
- **Sheaf discriminants** ([source](#pm-h-20)): For a positive-rank torsion-free sheaf $G$ put $$\label{eq:sheaf-discriminants} \begin{gathered} \mu(G)=\frac{d_0(G)}{r(G)},\qquad \xi_G=\left\langle\frac{c_0(G)}{r(G)},\frac{c_0(G)}{r(G)}\right\rangle -\frac{2w_0(G)}{r(G)},\\ \delta_G^T=\left(\frac{d_T(G)}{r(G)}\right)^2 -\frac{2w_T(G)}{r(G)}. \end{gathered}$$
- **Projected tilt discriminant** ([source](#eq:projected-discriminant)): For arbitrary derived objects the projected discriminant is $$\label{eq:projected-discriminant} \Delta(E)=d_b(E)^2-2r(E)w_b(E) =d_{B_0}(E)^2-2r(E)w_{B_0}(E).$$
- **Shifted derived dual** ([source](#pm-h-22)): To treat negative rank we use the shifted derived dual $\mathbb D(E)=R\mathcal Hom(E,\mathcal O_X)[1]$.
- **Wall defect and comparison quantity** ([source](#pm-h-35)): At slope zero define the defect and the nonnegative comparison quantity $$\label{eq:wall-defect} D_a(E)=z_b(E)-\frac{a^2d_b(E)}6,\qquad S_a(E)=\frac{\Delta(E)}{d_b(E)} =d_b(E)-\frac{a^2r(E)^2}{d_b(E)}.$$
- **Ordinary-character quadratic form** ([source](#eq:quadratic-form)): Define $$\label{eq:quadratic-form} Q_{\alpha,\beta}(v)= \alpha(v_1^2-2v_0v_2)+\beta(3v_0v_3-v_1v_2) +2v_2^2-3v_1v_3.$$

      "exact_definition": "For a positive-rank torsion-free sheaf $G$ put $$\\label{eq:sheaf-discriminants}\n\\begin{gathered}\n \\mu(G)=\\frac{d_0(G)}{r(G)},\\qquad\n \\xi_G=\\left\\langle\\frac{c_0(G)}{r(G)},\\frac{c_0(G)}{r(G)}\\right\\rangle\n             -\\frac{2w_0(G)}{r(G)},\\\\\n \\delta_G^T=\\left(\\frac{d_T(G)}{r(G)}\\right)^2\n             -\\frac{2w_T(G)}{r(G)}.\n\\end{gathered}$$",
      "interpretation": "The full sheaf discriminant and its polarized twisted counterpart record different amounts of first-character information.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "Sheaf discriminants",
      "scope": "Positive-rank torsion-free sheaves with the fixed polarization."
    },

          "source_id": "PM-055.2"
        }
      ],

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-common-sector-volume-threshold | t | ≥ | $T$ | A common open sector of real twists and ample directions for both prescribed charges. |
| q-uncorrected-tilt-tail-threshold | a | > | $R_*$ | Uniform in the object and every real scalar twist along the fixed polarization. |
| q-quadratic-parabolic-boundary | \alpha | > | $\frac{\beta^2+R_*^2}{2}$ | All slopes, including the infinite-slope denominator boundary. |
| q-physical-volume-convention | t | = | $\sqrt3a$ | The ordinary physical charge and the independent strong tilt inequality. |
| q-ambient-line-twist-phase-distance | d(\mathcal R_R,\mathcal R_R\otimes\mathcal O_Y(\mathbf e)) | ≤ | $\sum_h\frac{d_h\|e_h\|}{\pi Rs_h}$ | Integral multidegree twists and the continuous volume family. |
| q-deformation-phase-tolerance | \epsilon | < | $1/8$ | Strict slicing-distance tolerance for the large-volume real-sector deformation. |
| q-scalar-heat-kernel-bound | k_s(x,y) | ≤ | $C s^{-n/2}$ | The scalar heat kernel in the stated short-time interval. |
| q-subsheaf-section-bound | h^0(Y,J) | ≤ | $Cq\bigl(1+\mu(G)_+\bigr)^{\dim Y}$ | The subsheaf rank and ambient positive slope; the constant depends only on the geometric families. |
| q-matrix-spectral-dimension-bound | \dim U | ≤ | $C\int_S\operatorname{tr}(P^2)$ | Independent of the bundle, rank, metric connection, and bounded nonnegative matrix potential. |
| q-frobenius-presentation-generator-count | N_{p,L} | ≤ | $C_0p^3$ | Both rounding-line choices and the bounded scalar-twist normalization. |
| q-frobenius-maximal-slope-bound | \mu_{\max}(F^*G) | ≤ | $p\mu(G)+c_Y(r-1)$ | The gap constant is fixed by the chosen model and polarization; the error depends on the fixed sheaf rank. |
| q-wall-transport-tail-threshold | R_0 | = | $A+\frac{3C}{A}$ | An explicit tail threshold once the apex volume and defect constant are supplied. |
| q-wall-transport-global-correction | k_0 | = | $\max\left\{C+\frac{A^2}{6},\frac{CR_0}{A}\right\}$ | Uniform over finite-slope zero-slope semistable objects and all real scalar twists. |
| q-exceptional-third-character-ratio | \frac{\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_3^B(i_*F)}{H^2\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_1^B(i_*F)} | ≤ | $\frac1{24}$ | Independent of the sheaf, its rank, and the scalar twist. |
| q-exceptional-uncorrected-volume-boundary | a | ≥ | $1/2$ | The boundary is included for these objects; this is a specialized threshold rather than the general threefold threshold. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-full-numerical-embedding** [claim](#stmt-lem-full-numerical-embedding) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-full-numerical-embedding): Numerical Riemann–Roch, Hodge index, and Euler-pairing adjunction. -> A product-projective embedding with injective numerical pushforward.
- **step-elliptic-scaled-support** [claim](#stmt-lem-elliptic-scaled-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-elliptic-scaled-support): Product support induction and summation of omitted-factor quadratic forms in scaled coordinates. -> Volume-independent support on the elliptic-product coordinate lattice.
- **step-weighted-projective-descent** [claim](#stmt-prop-weighted-ambient) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-weighted-ambient): Successive finite quotients, explicit pullback coordinate comparison, and continuous deformation of weights. -> Weighted ambient stability with scaled support and line-twist phase bounds.
- **step-geometric-cohomology-range** [claim](#stmt-lem-geometric-range) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-geometric-range): Point-object phase vanishing, Serre duality, and minimal free complexes. -> Cohomological and aisle bounds with torsion-free bottom and zero-dimensional phase-one top cohomology.
- **step-primitive-open-charge-stability** [claim](#stmt-lem-primitive-point) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-primitive-point): Perturb the charge of a hypothetical stable proper factor independently inside a phase-window heart. -> Stability of the primitive-class object.
- **step-uniform-ambient-deformation** [claim](#stmt-lem-uniform-ambient-deformation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-uniform-ambient-deformation): Scaled coefficient estimates and support-property deformation with a strict phase-distance bound. -> Uniform corrected ambient lifts on a real sector and full open charge neighborhoods.
- **step-geometric-prescribed-restriction** [claim](#stmt-prop-geometric-prescribed-charges) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-geometric-prescribed-charges): Verify truncated cofiltration and Bayer bounds, apply finite-map restriction, and transfer ambient support through injective pushforward. -> Exact geometric prescribed charges with full numerical support and compatible ordinary surface slicings.
- **step-surface-heart-identification** [claim](#stmt-lem-surface-geometric-heart) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-surface-geometric-heart): Compare bounded hearts and identify their torsion pairs from imaginary-charge signs and quotient or subsheaf closure. -> The surface slope-tilt heart, including the nonpositive boundary.
- **step-weighted-cohomology-slopes** [claim](#stmt-lem-heart-cohomology-slopes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-heart-cohomology-slopes): Restrict outer cohomology and slope factors to smooth sections, then sum their inequalities with positive real weights. -> Opposite slope gaps for outer cohomology sheaves in the constructed heart.
- **step-first-tilt-phase-range** [claim](#stmt-lem-first-tilt-phase-range) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-first-tilt-phase-range): Exclude extreme phase factors by truncation and slope Hom-vanishing, then compare adjacent bounded hearts. -> A torsion pair in the first tilt recovering the constructed heart.
- **step-phase-one-boundary** [claim](#stmt-lem-phase-one-first-tilt) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-phase-one-first-tilt): Choose sections away from finite support and contradict a nonzero negative cohomology sheaf through weighted surface slopes. -> The object is a zero-dimensional sheaf.
- **step-physical-denominator-boundary** [claim](#stmt-lem-physical-zero-denominator) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-physical-zero-denominator): Use the slope torsion pair, real-ample Bogomolov–Gieseker, Hodge index, and effective curve cycles. -> Nonnegative denominator and the precise imaginary-charge equality case.
- **step-exact-double-tilt-heart** [claim](#stmt-prop-exact-double-tilt-heart) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-exact-double-tilt-heart): Compare the phase-cut and physical-slope torsion pairs, excluding their boundary mismatches. -> Equality of the constructed ordinary heart and standard double tilt.
- **step-common-prescribed-charge-sector** [claim](#stmt-thm-geometric-sector) / [proof](#sec:heart): Combine prescribed-charge restriction with ordinary-heart identification and intersect the charge neighborhoods. -> Both exact large-volume charges on a common real sector with a common threshold.
- **step-tilt-denominator-criterion** [claim](#stmt-lem-tilt-zero-denominator) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-tilt-zero-denominator): Analyze ordinary cohomology through the first torsion pair and use charge additivity in subobject–quotient sequences. -> Zero-charge classification and the finite-slope subobject criterion.
- **step-reduced-tilt-support** [claim](#stmt-lem-tilt-support-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-tilt-support-bound): Solve the projected discriminant inequality for rank and use the second tilt discriminant to control the full divisor norm. -> Uniform control of reduced numerical characters over real twists.
- **step-finite-stable-tilt-factors** [claim](#stmt-lem-finite-tilt-factors) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-tilt-factors): Split equal-slope factors, absorb point defects, and bound factor denominators below using rank integrality and support. -> Finite stable-factor filtrations and equal-slope extension semistability.
- **step-open-semicircle-transport** [claim](#stmt-lem-tilt-semicircle) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-tilt-semicircle): Compute the twist along the numerical arc and prove semistability is open and closed there using finite stable filtrations. -> Semistability on the whole open arc and a slope-zero apex.
- **step-real-tilt-duality** [claim](#stmt-lem-tilt-duality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-tilt-duality): Pass rational duality to real stable parameters by continuity, then extend through finite stable filtrations. -> Mirror-twist semistable truncation and the corrected character transformation.
- **step-restriction-variance** [claim](#stmt-lem-restriction-variance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-restriction-variance): Normalize the restriction discriminant inequality and use the weighted variance identity. -> Restriction variance and controlled increases of numerical moments.
- **step-uniform-smooth-families** [claim](#stmt-lem-uniform-sections) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-uniform-sections): Use vanishing and Bertini to choose smooth families, then impose finite open conditions and very-generality inside analytic neighborhoods. -> Uniform smooth geometry with exact and torsion-free restrictions where required.
- **step-uniform-heat** [claim](#stmt-lem-uniform-heat) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-uniform-heat): Uniform Nash inequality, scalar semigroup bounds, and Kato domination. -> Scalar and rank-independent connection heat bounds.
- **step-subsheaf-sections** [claim](#stmt-lem-subsheaf-sections) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-subsheaf-sections): Hermitian–Einstein metric, Bochner heat comparison, and the rank of the evaluation operator. -> Section bounds depending on subsheaf rank rather than ambient rank.
- **step-matrix-spectral** [claim](#stmt-lem-matrix-spectral) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-matrix-spectral): Min–max, heat-semigroup factorization, and a fiberwise spectral split retaining the matrix trace. -> A dimension bound by integrated squared potential with rank-independent constant.
- **step-negative-surface-cohomology** [claim](#stmt-prop-negative-surface-cohomology) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-negative-surface-cohomology): Separate large numerical deviation from the small-deviation case; use Riemann–Roch in the former and curvature-energy spectral control in the latter. -> First cohomology controlled by deviation and discriminant energy without a bare-rank term.
- **step-frobenius-presentation** [claim](#stmt-lem-frobenius-presentation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-frobenius-presentation): Spread fixed line-bundle presentations and regularity bounds, then apply projection formula and Riemann–Roch. -> Uniform presentation twists, globally generated kernels, and cubic generator-count bounds.
- **step-frobenius-section-transfer** [claim](#stmt-cor-frobenius-section-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-frobenius-section-transfer): Finite duality and presentation-induced Hom injectivity from negative-cohomology vanishing. -> Frobenius section bounds transferred to fixed twists of the original complex.
- **step-restriction-image-cancellation** [claim](#stmt-lem-restriction-image) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-restriction-image): Control the tilt evaluation image numerically, produce almost all evaluation-source sections from its kernel, and cancel their dimensions after restriction. -> Residual restriction-image dimension bounded by excess independently of source dimension.
- **step-residual-sections** [claim](#stmt-cor-residual-sections) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-residual-sections): Telescope restriction images from a fixed initial vanishing and use surface first-cohomology control for the negative decomposition. -> Excess-proportional fixed-twist sections and the required negative-cohomology vanishings.
- **step-fixed-sheaf-spreading** [claim](#stmt-lem-fixed-sheaf-spreading) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-fixed-sheaf-spreading): Bound possible destabilizing subsheaves by finite-type wedge-map parameter spaces and remove the constructible destabilizing locus. -> Localized models preserving finitely many ordinary semistability requirements.
- **step-frobenius-slope-gap** [claim](#stmt-lem-frobenius-slope-gap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-frobenius-slope-gap): Use the canonical connection and Cartier descent to rule out excessive adjacent Harder–Narasimhan slope gaps. -> A model-dependent maximal-slope bound for Frobenius pullback.
- **step-fixed-apex-excess-bound** [claim](#stmt-prop-apex) / [proof](#sec:apex): Separate narrow slope tails from residual complexes, transfer section bounds through Frobenius, control dual cohomology, and take the Riemann–Roch limit. -> A fixed-apex defect bound proportional to denominator excess, uniform in objects and scalar twists.
- **step-apex-wall-transport** [claim](#stmt-prop-apex-transport) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-apex-transport): Differentiate defect comparisons along zero-slope branches and replace violating objects at walls by stable factors of strictly smaller discrete discriminant. -> Explicit all-volume correction and uncorrected tail from the apex input.
- **step-uniform-strong-inequality** [claim](#stmt-thm-uniform-inequality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-apex-transport-2): Compare denominator excess with the transport quantity, apply the transport proposition, undo polarization reductions, and enlarge the correction rationally. -> The rational corrected all-volume inequality and independent uncorrected large-volume threshold.
- **step-quadratic-all-slope-tail** [claim](#stmt-cor-quadratic-tail) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-apex-transport-3): Expand the quadratic form in twisted coordinates, handle zero denominator directly, and transport finite slope to its semicircle apex. -> Nonnegativity of the ordinary-character quadratic form in the parabolic tail region.
- **step-exceptional-surface-bound** [claim](#stmt-lem-exceptional-surface-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-exceptional-surface-bound): Deduce surface slope semistability from tilt-semistable pushforward and combine surface discriminants with divisor Grothendieck–Riemann–Roch. -> Uniform third-character ratio and specialized corrected and uncorrected bounds.

          "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be a smooth projective threefold. Rational Chern characters identify its numerical Grothendieck group with $$\\label{eq:full-numerical-lattice}\n K_{\\mathrm{num}}(X)_\\mathbb Q\\simeq\n \\mathbb Q\\oplus N^1(X)_\\mathbb Q\\oplus N_1(X)_\\mathbb Q\\oplus\\mathbb Q.$$ Products of numerical divisor classes span this space. Given a real ample class $H_*$, there are very ample line bundles $L_1,\\ldots,L_q$ whose classes form a basis of $N^1(X)_\\mathbb R$ and positive numbers $s_1,\\ldots,s_q$ with $H_*=\\sum_hs_hL_h$. For the product embedding $$\\label{eq:product-embedding}\n i:X\\hookrightarrow Y=\\prod_{h=1}^q\\mathbb P^{d_h},\n \\qquad d_h=h^0(X,L_h)-1,$$ the pushforward $i_*:K_{\\mathrm{num}}(X)_\\mathbb R\\to K_{\\mathrm{num}}(Y)_\\mathbb R$ is injective.",

        }

      "output": "A product-projective embedding with injective numerical pushforward.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-full-numerical-embedding"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-elliptic-scaled-support",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-elliptic-scaled-support",
          "exact_quote": "Let $C$ be an elliptic curve, let $n\\ge1$, and fix positive rational numbers $\\rho_1,\\ldots,\\rho_n$. For rational $R\\ge1$, put $w_r=R\\rho_r$ and let $H_r$ be the point-divisor class from the $r$th factor of $C^n$. The product stability condition with charge $$Z_{\\mathbf w}(u)=-\\int_{C^n}e^{-i\\sum_rw_rH_r}\\mathop{\\mathrm{ch}}(u)$$ has support on the coordinate lattice $$x_I(u)=H_I\\mathop{\\mathrm{ch}}_{n-|I|}(u),\\qquad\n H_I=\\prod_{r\\in I}H_r,\\qquad I\\subset\\{1,\\ldots,n\\}.$$ More precisely, set $y_I=(\\prod_{r\\in I}w_r)x_I$. In these coordinates, $$Z_{\\mathbf w}(u)=\\widehat Z_n(y(u)),\\qquad\n \\widehat Z_n(y)=-\\sum_I(-i)^{|I|}y_I.$$ For a fixed Euclidean norm there is $C_n>0$, independent of $R$, such that $$\\|y(u)\\|\\le C_n|Z_{\\mathbf w}(u)|\n \\qquad(u\\text{ the class of a semistable object}).$$ The constants can be chosen uniformly when the positive ratios $\\rho_r$ vary in a compact set. Point sheaves are stable of phase $1$.",
          "source_id": "PM-055.2"
        }
      ],

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-weighted-ambient",
          "exact_quote": "Let $Y=\\prod_{h=1}^q\\mathbb P^{d_h}$, put $\\xi_h=c_1(\\mathcal O_h(1))$, and fix $s_h>0$. There is a continuous family of numerical stability conditions $(U_R,\\mathcal R_R)$ for $R\\ge1$ with $$U_R(u)=-\\int_Ye^{-iR\\sum_hs_h\\xi_h}\\mathop{\\mathrm{ch}}(u).$$ All point sheaves are stable of phase $1$. Write $\\mathop{\\mathrm{ch}}(u)=\\sum_{\\mathbf j}u_{\\mathbf j}\\xi^{\\mathbf j}$, where $0\\le j_h\\le d_h$, and set $$\\label{eq:ambient-scaled-coordinates}\n (M_Ru)_{\\mathbf j}=\n u_{\\mathbf j}\\prod_h(Rs_h)^{d_h-j_h}.$$ For a fixed Euclidean norm there is $D>0$, independent of $R$, such that $$\\label{eq:ambient-support}\n \\|M_Ru\\|\\le D|U_R(u)|\\qquad\n (u\\text{ the class of an }\\mathcal R_R\\text{-semistable object}).$$ For every $\\mathbf e\\in\\mathbb Z^q$, $$\\label{eq:ambient-twist-distance}\n d(\\mathcal R_R,\\mathcal R_R\\otimes\\mathcal O_Y(\\mathbf e))\n \\le\\sum_h\\frac{d_h|e_h|}{\\pi Rs_h}.$$",

        }

          "anchor": "stmt-thm-geometric-sector",
          "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be a smooth projective complex threefold with $K_X\\simeq\\mathcal O_X$. At twists $B$ and $-B$ respectively their coordinates are related by $$\\label{eq:tilt-dual-characters}\n (r,d,w,z)(\\widetilde E)\n   =\\bigl(-r(E),d_B(E),-w_B(E),z_B(E)+\\operatorname{length}(T_0)/h\\bigr).$$ Moreover $\\widetilde E=\\tau^{\\le0}\\mathbb D(E)$ for the ordinary cohomological truncation.",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-restriction-variance",
          "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be a slope-semistable torsion-free sheaf of rank $r>0$ on $X$. For a very general smooth $S\\in|H|$, restrict $G$ and refine its slope Harder–Narasimhan filtration into slope-stable torsion-free factors $G_i$, with ranks $r_i$ and normalized slopes $\\mu_i$. Then $$\\label{eq:restriction-variance}\n \\sum_i r_i\\bigl(\\mu_i-\\mu(G)\\bigr)^2\\le r\\xi_G.$$ The same assertion holds for a slope-semistable torsion-free sheaf on $S$, restricted to a very general smooth $C_m\\in|mH|_S$, for every positive integer $m$.*\n\n*More generally, restrict a finite filtration with semistable torsion-free factors and refine each restricted factor separately. From $X$ to $S$, for any fixed divisor twist $T$ on $X$, restricted to $S$, the increases in $\\sum_i r_i\\mu_T(G_i)^2$ and in $\\sum_i r_i\\delta_{G_i}^T$ are equal and lie between zero and the original sum $\\sum_G r(G)\\xi_G$. From $S$ to $C_m$, the same upper bound holds for the increase of the second slope moment, using any fixed divisor twist on $S$ and its restriction to $C_m$. The concatenated filtration need not be the Harder–Narasimhan filtration of the whole restriction.",
          "source_id": "PM-055.2"
        }

      "depends_on": [

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-uniform-sections",
          "exact_quote": "There is an integer $m>0$, depending only on $X,H$, such that every smooth $S\\in|H|$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:surface-fixed-m}\n h^0(S,\\mathcal O_S(mH))=\\chi(\\mathcal O_S(mH)),\n \\qquad \\mu(K_S)-m\\le-1,$$ and $H^0(X,\\mathcal O_X(mH))\\to H^0(S,\\mathcal O_S(mH))$ is surjective. Restrictions of a specified finite collection of torsion-free sheaves may also be required to remain torsion-free.
