<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-exact-charges-real-sector**: The ordinary and square-root-Todd charges admit geometric numerical stability conditions with full numerical support throughout a common open real sector above a common volume threshold. The ordinary charge uses the standard double-tilt heart.
- **invariant-corrected-bound-and-uncorrected-tail**: Finite-slope tilt-semistable objects of slope zero satisfy the corrected strong inequality at every positive volume and the uncorrected strong inequality above a fixed threshold. The constants depend on the polarized variety and rational transverse twist, independently of the object and the twist parameter along the polarization.
- **invariant-quadratic-parabolic-tail**: The ordinary Chern vector of every nonzero semistable object satisfies the generalized quadratic inequality above the stated parabolic boundary, including the zero-denominator regime.
- **invariant-fixed-apex-excess**: At a fixed volume, the strong-inequality defect is bounded by the denominator excess over the volume times the absolute rank. This bound introduces no additional bare rank term and holds uniformly in the object and twist parameter.
- **invariant-reduced-exceptional-surface**: A torsion-free sheaf of positive rank on the specified smooth reduced exceptional divisor has the stated uniform third-character bound when its pushforward is tilt-semistable of slope zero. The resulting correction and uncorrected volume threshold are independent of the sheaf, its rank and the twist parameter.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-nontrivial-canonical-bundle**: Apply the prescribed ordinary and square-root-Todd charge construction to every smooth projective threefold. -> The prescribed-charge theorem assumes trivial canonical bundle; the broader scope of the independent tilt inequality does not extend that construction.
- **anti-use-uncorrected-small-volume**: Remove the correction from the strong tilt inequality at every positive volume. -> Stable contracted-divisor counterexamples have positive defect in the stated bounded volume interval.
- **anti-use-reduced-coordinates-as-full-support**: Treat the tilt support estimate for reduced character coordinates as full numerical support. -> The estimate does not control every numerical Chern-character class and therefore does not establish support on the full numerical Grothendieck group.
- **anti-use-todd-heart-identification**: Assign the ordinary double-tilt heart to the Todd-corrected charge using the ordinary-heart comparison. -> The equality of hearts is proved only for the ordinary character factor. The Todd-corrected construction retains its separately constructed geometric heart.
- **anti-use-numerical-positivity-as-semistability**: Accept an object as tilt-semistable because its Chern vector satisfies the quadratic inequality. -> The corollary gives a necessary numerical inequality for semistable objects. Its statement provides no converse deciding semistability from numerical positivity.
- **anti-use-nonreduced-divisor-multiples**: Replace the reduced exceptional divisor by a nonreduced multiple to obtain stable violations at arbitrarily large volume. -> The displayed exact sequence supplies a destabilizing subsheaf of the nonreduced structure sheaf. Character calculations alone do not establish its semistability.
- **anti-use-specialization-as-uniform-finite-certificate**: Treat a finite collection of reductions or numerical samples as verification of the uniform strong inequality. -> The proof chooses spreading data after fixing the object and uses a limit through unbounded prime characteristics. Agreement on finitely many reductions cannot certify the quantified result or the disappearance of object-dependent errors.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The geometric stability constructions and uniform inequalities are not finite constructive results with an executable input-output contract. The charge construction uses support-property deformation and restriction of slicings; the numerical proof chooses spreading data for each fixed object and passes to a Frobenius limit through unbounded prime characteristics. The source states: “For each fixed object, we reduce finitely many sheaves and maps to positive characteristic.” Explicit character identities can be checked separately, but such checks do not decide semistability, construct the asserted slicings, or certify the object-independent thresholds.

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-prescribed-charge-canonical-bundle** (hypothesis): The exact prescribed-charge construction requires a smooth projective complex threefold with trivial canonical bundle. The independent tilt inequality does not require that canonical-bundle hypothesis. Blocked use: ['Applying the prescribed-charge construction to a threefold with nontrivial canonical bundle merely because the uniform tilt inequality applies.']
- **limit-common-sector-locality** (hypothesis): The common volume threshold applies within the constructed open neighborhoods of the chosen twist and ample direction. The theorem does not supply a common threshold over the entire divisor space and ample cone. Blocked use: ['Extending the common threshold to arbitrary twists and ample directions outside the constructed sector.']
- **limit-todd-heart-comparison** (formal_scope): The standard double-tilt heart is identified only for the ordinary character. The square-root-Todd charge retains its separately constructed geometric heart. Blocked use: ['Assigning the ordinary double-tilt heart to the square-root-Todd charge without an additional heart comparison.']
- **limit-small-volume-correction** (counterexample_scope): The uncorrected strong inequality is a tail assertion. Stable counterexamples at small volume prevent removing the correction at every positive volume. Blocked use: ['Using the uncorrected bound below the established threshold.', 'Treating translation of the scalar twist as enlargement of the counterexample volume range.']
- **limit-fixed-polarization-rational-transverse-data** (hypothesis): The uniform strong inequality fixes an integral ample polarization and a rational transverse twist. Uniformity in the real scalar twist does not assert uniformity over changing polarization directions or arbitrary transverse twists. Blocked use: ['Reusing the same inequality constants while changing the polarization or transverse twist.', 'Replacing rational transverse data by arbitrary real data in the discrete wall-descent argument.']
- **limit-zero-slope-antecedent** (hypothesis): The strong third-character bounds require finite-slope tilt semistability and slope zero in the specified first-tilt heart. Arbitrary finite slopes are treated through the semicircle argument and quadratic formulation. Blocked use: ['Applying the zero-slope third-character inequality directly to an arbitrary derived object or an object of nonzero tilt slope.']
- **limit-quadratic-necessary-condition** (formal_scope): The quadratic inequality is a necessary consequence of semistability in the stated parabolic region. The source gives no converse certifying semistability from a nonnegative quadratic value. Blocked use: ['Accepting a Chern vector as semistable solely because the quadratic form is nonnegative.', 'Using the corollary at or below its strict parabolic boundary.']
- **limit-threefold-numerical-embedding** (formal_scope): The divisor-product spanning argument that makes numerical pushforward injective uses dimension three. It is not an injectivity theorem for arbitrary-dimensional embeddings. Blocked use: ['Transferring full numerical support through an arbitrary-dimensional product embedding without proving numerical injectivity.']
- **limit-scaled-positive-weight-support** (hypothesis): The ambient support argument uses positive weights and volume-scaled coordinates. Uniformity of elliptic-product support is stated on compact sets of positive ratios. Blocked use: ['Keeping the same support constant when positive ratios degenerate outside a compact positive set.', 'Reading the scaled-coordinate support estimate as a volume-independent bound in unscaled coordinates.']
- **limit-full-charge-openness** (hypothesis): The primitive-class stability criterion requires semistability in a neighborhood open in the full central-charge space, together with support on a finite-rank lattice. Blocked use: ['Inferring stability from persistence of semistability only along a restricted parameter family.', 'Omitting primitivity or the support hypothesis.']
- **limit-restriction-cofiltration-gates** (hypothesis): Inducing a slicing by finite-map restriction requires the stated Bayer properties, cohomological aisle bounds, and truncation bound. A truncated resolution alone does not establish these requirements. Blocked use: ['Restricting an ambient stability condition without checking all line-bundle shifts and the remainder bound.', 'Assuming that numerical equality of restricted charges provides Harder–Narasimhan filtrations.']
- **limit-slope-boundary-and-point-defects** (edge_case): The tilt conventions retain the strict-positive/nonpositive boundary and assign infinite slope at zero denominator. Zero-dimensional quotients do not destroy stability under the source's subobject–quotient convention. Blocked use: ['Replacing the stated stability convention by strict subobject–object comparison.', 'Discarding zero-charge point objects or dividing by a vanishing tilt denominator.']
- **limit-reduced-support-not-full-support** (formal_scope): The reduced tilt estimate controls rank, the full first character, and a polarized degree of the second character. It does not establish support on the full numerical Grothendieck group. Blocked use: ['Using the reduced tilt estimate as the full numerical support property.', 'Assuming that all curve-class and third-character directions are controlled by the reduced charge.']
- **limit-open-semicircle-endpoints** (edge_case): Semistability transport is proved on the open numerical semicircle. The statement does not include its endpoints at vanishing volume. Blocked use: ['Extending semicircle semistability to the endpoints without a separate boundary argument.']
- **limit-duality-point-correction** (edge_case): Shifted derived duality produces a semistable ordinary truncation and a zero-dimensional defect. The defect contributes to the third character and cannot generally be omitted. Blocked use: ['Identifying the entire shifted derived dual with the mirror semistable object.', 'Treating the third character as unchanged when the point defect is present.']
- **limit-very-general-restriction-filtrations** (nonconstructive): The restriction estimates use suitable very general divisors and finitely many exactness conditions for the specified sheaves. Refining restricted factors separately does not identify the concatenated filtration with the whole restriction's Harder–Narasimhan filtration. Blocked use: ['Applying the variance estimate to an arbitrary singular or unsuitable section.', 'Assuming the factorwise concatenation is automatically the Harder–Narasimhan filtration.', 'Treating existence of a very general section as a supplied finite selection algorithm.']
- **limit-analytic-cohomology-constant-order** (hypothesis): The surface cohomology threshold is chosen from the upper twist bound and fixed geometry. The cohomology constant may then depend on the chosen volume and finite twist set, while remaining independent of the sheaf collection and its ranks. Blocked use: ['Claiming uniformity over unrestricted twist sets.', 'Replacing the error-proportional cohomology bound by a bound containing an additional bare rank term.']
- **limit-spectral-form-domain** (hypothesis): The spectral dimension estimate concerns a bounded nonnegative selfadjoint bundle endomorphism and a finite-dimensional subspace satisfying quadratic-form domination on a surface in the chosen smooth family. Blocked use: ['Applying the estimate to an arbitrary matrix without the geometric operator and form-domain hypotheses.', 'Replacing the matrix trace by a scalar operator-norm estimate that introduces an uncontrolled rank factor.']
- **limit-frobenius-section-transfer-vanishing** (hypothesis): The Frobenius section-transfer bound requires the specified ordinary cohomological range and the negative-cohomology vanishing at the fixed presentation twist. Blocked use: ['Applying section transfer to a complex with cohomology outside the specified range.', 'Dropping the vanishing needed for injectivity of the presentation-induced Hom map.']
- **limit-fixed-object-spreading-and-limit** (nonconstructive): Spreading and localization are performed after fixing the individual object and finitely many sheaves and maps. Tilt semistability is not required on the reductions. Object-dependent errors disappear only in the Frobenius limit. Blocked use: ['Claiming a single object-independent localization preserving tilt semistability.', 'Using finitely many characteristic reductions as a certificate of the universal inequality.', 'Allowing object-dependent coefficients to survive in the leading limit.']
- **limit-wall-descent-effectivity** (nonconstructive): Wall transport assumes a uniform fixed-apex inequality and uses strict descent of a discrete discriminant. Termination of hypothetical violating replacements does not provide a procedure locating walls or computing stable factors. Blocked use: ['Treating supplied numerical apex constants as valid without establishing their uniform inequality.', 'Claiming an executable semistability decision algorithm from the wall-descent proof.']
- **limit-exceptional-reduced-surface** (hypothesis): The exceptional-surface bound requires a smooth integral divisor with the specified normal polarization and a positive-rank torsion-free sheaf whose pushforward is tilt-semistable of slope zero. Surface slope semistability alone is not the stated sufficient hypothesis. Blocked use: ['Applying the bound to arbitrary divisor-supported sheaves.', 'Omitting semistability of the pushforward.', 'Applying the reduced-surface argument to arbitrary nonreduced divisor multiples.']

          "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be a smooth projective complex threefold, let $H$ be an ample integral divisor, and let $i:D\\hookrightarrow X$ be a smooth integral divisor such that $H|_D=-D|_D$. If $F$ is a torsion-free sheaf of positive rank on $D$ and $i_*F$ is $\\nu_{a,b}$-semistable of slope zero, then $$\\frac{\\mathop{\\mathrm{ch}}_3^B(i_*F)}{H^2\\mathop{\\mathrm{ch}}_1^B(i_*F)}\\le\\frac1{24}.\n \\label{eq:exceptional-uniform}$$ Consequently these objects satisfy the corrected strong inequality with $\\Gamma=H^2/24$ for every $a>0$, and the uncorrected strong inequality for every $a\\ge1/2$.           "exact_quote": "Replacing $D$ by $nD$ in the character calculation does not produce a stable counterexample at arbitrarily large volume. Under the hypotheses of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:exceptional-surface-bound\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:exceptional-surface-bound\">36</a>, the exact sequence $$0\\longrightarrow\\mathcal O_D(-(n-1)D)\\longrightarrow\\mathcal O_{nD}\n \\longrightarrow\\mathcal O_{(n-1)D}\\longrightarrow0$$ lies in every first-tilt heart. For $B_0=0$ and $b=n/2$, its three slopes are $(n-1)/2$, $0$, and $-1/2$, respectively. Thus $\\mathcal O_{nD}$ is unstable for every $a>0$ when $n>1$. The uniform estimate above concerns sheaves on the reduced divisor; its proof uses surface semistability, rather than the character of an arbitrary multiple.",
          "source_id": "PM-055.2"
        }

          "anchor": "stmt-thm-geometric-sector",
          "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be a smooth projective complex threefold with $K_X\\simeq\\mathcal O_X$. For any $B_*\\in N^1(X)_{\\mathbb R}$ and $H_*\\in\\operatorname{Amp}(X)$ there are open neighborhoods $V$ of $B_*$ and $W$ of $H_*$ and one $T>0$ such that, for all $B\\in V$, $H\\in W$ and $t\\geq T$, the following hold.*\n\n1.  *$(Z_{B,tH},\\mathcal A_{B,tH})$ is a geometric numerical stability condition with support on $K_{\\mathrm{num}}(X)_{\\mathbb R}$.*\n\n2.  *There is a geometric numerical stability condition, with support on the same full numerical space, whose charge is exactly $$Z^{\\mathrm{LV}}_{B,tH}(E)\n     =-\\int_Xe^{-B-itH}\\mathop{\\mathrm{ch}}(E)\\sqrt{\\mathop{\\mathrm{td}}(X)},\n     \\qquad \\sqrt{\\mathop{\\mathrm{td}}(X)}=1+\\frac{c_2(X)}{24}.$$ Its heart is the one constructed in Section <a href=\"#sec:ambient\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:ambient\">2</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-055.2"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "pm-h-6",
          "exact_quote": "For each fixed object, we reduce finitely many sheaves and maps to positive characteristic.",
          "source_id": "PM-055.2"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "eq:rr-frobenius-limit",
          "exact_quote": "Riemann–Roch identifies the limit on the left: $$\\label{eq:rr-frobenius-limit}\n \\lim_{p\\to\\infty}p^{-3}\\chi(F^*E\\otimes L_p)=h z_B(E).$$ Here is a way to compare varying characteristics without identifying their cohomology rings. On a smooth fiber, $\\mathop{\\mathrm{ch}}_i(F^*E)=p^i\\mathop{\\mathrm{ch}}_i(E)$ by the splitting principle. The same formula holds for the Adams class $\\psi^p[E]$ in vector-bundle $K$-theory. Riemann–Roch thus identifies the Euler characteristic in <a href=\"#eq:rr-frobenius-limit\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:rr-frobenius-limit\">[eq:rr-frobenius-limit]</a> with that of $\\psi^p[E]\\otimes L_p$. This class is defined by exterior-power operations on a fixed perfect presentation and commutes with specialization. Its Euler characteristic is constant from the model to the complex fiber. On that fiber, write $c_1(L_p)=-pB+R_p$, where $R_p$ remains in a fixed bounded set of divisor classes. The degree-three leading term of $$\\int_X\\left(\\sum_{i=0}^3p^i\\mathop{\\mathrm{ch}}_i(E)\\right)\n                  e^{-pB+R_p}\\mathop{\\mathrm{td}}(X)$$ is $p^3\\mathop{\\mathrm{ch}}_3^B(E)$; all other terms are $O_E(p^2)$. This proves <a href=\"#eq:rr-frobenius-limit\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:rr-frobenius-limit\">[eq:rr-frobenius-limit]</a>, without a condition on $K_X$.",
          "source_id": "PM-055.2"
        }

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


Let <i>X</i> be a smooth projective complex threefold with trivial canonical bundle. We construct numerical Bridgeland stability conditions with the exact ordinary and square-root-Todd central charges at every sufficiently large volume. One volume threshold works on an open set of real twists and ample directions. The resulting stability conditions have the support property on the full numerical Grothendieck group and stable point sheaves. Separately, on every smooth projective complex threefold we prove a strong tilt inequality above a volume threshold uniform in the object and twist along a fixed polarization.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

A central charge assigns a complex number to each object of a derived category. A stability condition organizes the objects by its argument and requires every object to have a finite filtration by semistable factors. Bridgeland introduced this structure and its deformation theory (Bridgeland 2007) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Bridgeland2007](#ref-Bridgeland2007)]. On a projective threefold, the expected large-volume charges are exponential expressions in the Chern character. The prescribed-charge problem asks whether these particular expressions admit a compatible heart, finite semistable filtrations, and a support bound controlling the numerical classes of all semistable objects.

We construct stability conditions with the exact ordinary and square-root-Todd large-volume charges on every smooth projective complex threefold with trivial canonical bundle. One volume threshold works on an open set of real twists and real ample directions. The support property holds on the full numerical Grothendieck group, and the ordinary charge has the standard double-tilt heart. An independent argument proves strong tilt inequalities on every smooth projective complex threefold: a fixed rational correction works at every volume, and the correction vanishes beyond a threshold independent of the object and of the twist along a fixed polarization.

All varieties are over $\mathbb C$, and all Chern characters and intersection products are numerical. Write $N^1(X)_{\mathbb R}$ for the space of numerical real divisor classes, $\operatorname{Amp}(X)$ for its ample cone, and $N_1(X)_{\mathbb Q}$ for the space of numerical rational curve classes. A threefold is smooth, connected and projective. The assertions for a disjoint union follow by applying the results to its finitely many connected components and taking the largest constants. Write $D^{\mathrm b}(X)=D^{\mathrm b}\mathop{\mathrm{Coh}}(X)$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}^B(E)=e^{-B}\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}(E)$ for a real numerical divisor class $B$.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Prescribed charges and the full numerical group

The Euler pairing is $\chi(E,F)=\sum_j(-1)^j\dim\mathop{\mathrm{Ext}}^j(E,F)$, and we use $$K_{\mathrm{num}}(X)=K_0(X)/\{v:\chi(v,w)=0\text{ for every }w\in K_0(X)\}.$$ A numerical stability condition consists of a charge homomorphism $Z:K_{\mathrm{num}}(X)\to\mathbb C$ and a locally finite slicing $\mathcal P$ of $D^{\mathrm b}(X)$. A slicing gives the semistable objects of each real phase, with $Z(E)=m(E)e^{i\pi\phi}$, $m(E)>0$, on $\mathcal P(\phi)$, the shift and Hom-vanishing rules, and finite Harder–Narasimhan filtrations. Local finiteness requires the categories of sufficiently short phase intervals to have finite length. We require the support property of Kontsevich–Soibelman (Kontsevich and Soibelman 2008, sec. 1.2 and 2.1) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[KontsevichSoibelman2008](#ref-KontsevichSoibelman2008)] on the full real space $K_{\mathrm{num}}(X)_{\mathbb R}$: for a norm there is $C>0$ such that $\|[E]\|\leq C|Z(E)|$ for every semistable $E$. Its heart is $\mathcal P(0,1]$. We call a stability condition geometric if all point sheaves $\mathcal O_x$ are stable of phase $1$.

We specify the tilt conventions needed to state the heart in our theorem. For a real ample class $H$, a real divisor class $B$, and a coherent sheaf of positive rank put $$\mu_{H,B}(F)=\frac{H^2\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_1^B(F)}{H^3\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_0(F)},$$ and give a nonzero torsion sheaf slope $+\infty$. The slope Harder–Narasimhan filtration has semistable factors of strictly decreasing slopes; write $\mu^+$ and $\mu^-$ for its largest and smallest slopes. The slope torsion pair and its tilt are $$\mathcal T_{H,B}=\{F:\mu^-_{H,B}(F)>0\},\qquad
 \mathcal F_{H,B}=\{F:\mu^+_{H,B}(F)\leq0\},\qquad
 \mathcal B_{H,B}=\langle\mathcal F_{H,B}[1],\mathcal T_{H,B}\rangle,$$ with the zero sheaf in both subcategories. Here a heart is the abelian category specified by a bounded $t$-structure, $[1]$ is the cohomological shift, and brackets denote extension closure. Thus an object of $\mathcal B_{H,B}$ has ordinary cohomology only in degrees $-1,0$, in the indicated two subcategories.

<a id="eq:intro-physical"></a>
For real $B$, real ample $H$ and $t>0$, the ordinary physical charge is $$\label{eq:intro-physical}
 Z_{B,tH}(E)=
 -\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_3^B(E)+\frac{t^2}{2}H^2\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_1^B(E)
 +i\left(tH\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_2^B(E)-\frac{t^3}{6}H^3\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_0(E)\right).$$ The first tilt is unchanged if the polarization $H$ is replaced by $tH$. On this first tilt, use the second slope $$\nu_{B,tH}(E)=
 \frac{tH\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_2^B(E)-t^3H^3\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_0(E)/6}{t^2H^2\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_1^B(E)}.$$ Its denominator-zero value is $+\infty$. An object is semistable for this slope if every nonzero proper subobject in $\mathcal B_{H,B}$ has slope at most that of the quotient; stability uses a strict inequality. The Harder–Narasimhan property holds for these real ample parameters (Bayer, Macrì, and Stellari 2016, sec. 2) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[BMS2016](#ref-BMS2016)]. Let $\mathcal T'_{B,tH}$ have strictly positive smallest Harder–Narasimhan slope, and $\mathcal F'_{B,tH}$ have nonpositive largest slope. The standard double-tilt heart is $$\mathcal A_{B,tH}=
 \langle\mathcal F'_{B,tH}[1],\mathcal T'_{B,tH}\rangle.$$ These definitions use the same strict-positive/nonpositive boundary at both tilts.

<a id="stmt-thm-geometric-sector"></a>
<div id="thm:geometric-sector" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1** (Exact large-volume charges on a real sector). *Let $X$ be a smooth projective complex threefold with $K_X\simeq\mathcal O_X$. For any $B_*\in N^1(X)_{\mathbb R}$ and $H_*\in\operatorname{Amp}(X)$ there are open neighborhoods $V$ of $B_*$ and $W$ of $H_*$ and one $T>0$ such that, for all $B\in V$, $H\in W$ and $t\geq T$, the following hold.*

1.  *$(Z_{B,tH},\mathcal A_{B,tH})$ is a geometric numerical stability condition with support on $K_{\mathrm{num}}(X)_{\mathbb R}$.*

2.  *There is a geometric numerical stability condition, with support on the same full numerical space, whose charge is exactly $$Z^{\mathrm{LV}}_{B,tH}(E)
     =-\int_Xe^{-B-itH}\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}(E)\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{td}}(X)},
     \qquad \sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{td}}(X)}=1+\frac{c_2(X)}{24}.$$ Its heart is the one constructed in Section <a href="#sec:ambient" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:ambient">2</a>.*

</div>

Only triviality of $K_X$ is assumed; the additional vanishings $H^1(X,\mathcal O_X)=H^2(X,\mathcal O_X)=0$ in the strict Calabi–Yau convention are unnecessary. The quantifiers give one threshold for both charges throughout the same real sector. The two hearts are specified separately: the ordinary charge uses the double tilt defined above, while the Todd-corrected charge uses the heart constructed in Section <a href="#sec:ambient" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:ambient">2</a>.

The ordinary and square-root-Todd charges have large-volume antecedents in Bayer’s polynomial stability conditions (Bayer 2009, Theorem 3.2.2 and Section 4) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[BayerPolynomial2009](#ref-BayerPolynomial2009)]. Polynomial stability orders phases asymptotically as the volume tends to infinity. Our theorem constructs a numerical Bridgeland stability condition at each finite volume in the stated sector. The passage to finite volume must also control the charge kernel: on a threefold of Picard rank greater than one, the full numerical group contains classes invisible to the four intersection degrees along a single polarization.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Independent uniform tilt inequalities

The numerical problem concerns the third Chern character, which is absent from the classical Bogomolov–Gieseker inequality. Bayer–Macrì–Toda introduced the double-tilt construction and proposed a third-character inequality that would make it a source of stability conditions (Bayer, Macrì, and Toda 2014) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[BMT2014](#ref-BMT2014)]. Bayer–Macrì–Stellari established that inequality for abelian threefolds and Calabi–Yau threefolds of abelian type, and developed the discriminants, deformation and wall framework used below (Bayer, Macrì, and Stellari 2016) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[BMS2016](#ref-BMS2016)]. For this part fix an ample integral divisor $H$ and a rational numerical divisor $B_0$.

<a id="eq:intro-tilt"></a>
For $a>0$, $b\in\mathbb R$ and $B=B_0+bH$, the tilt slope is $$\label{eq:intro-tilt}
 \nu_{a,b}(E)=
 \frac{H\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_2^B(E)-\tfrac12a^2H^3\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_0(E)}
      {H^2\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_1^B(E)},$$ with value $+\infty$ when the denominator is zero. We use the same subobject–quotient comparison for tilt stability. At finite slope, semistability is equivalently the comparison with the slope of the object itself. The treatment of zero-dimensional quotients and the finite factor filtrations needed below is given in Section <a href="#sec:tilt" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:tilt">4</a>.

<a id="stmt-thm-uniform-inequality"></a>
<div id="thm:uniform-inequality" class="theorem">

<a id="eq:intro-tail"></a>
**Theorem 2** (Uniform strong inequality). *Let $X$ be a smooth projective complex threefold, $H$ an ample integral divisor, and $B_0\in N^1(X)_{\mathbb Q}$. There are constants $k\in\mathbb Q_{\geq0}$ and $R_*>0$, depending only on $(X,H,B_0)$, such that every finite-slope $\nu_{a,b}$-semistable $E\in\mathcal B_{H,B_0+bH}$ with $\nu_{a,b}(E)=0$ satisfies <a id="eq:intro-corrected"></a>$$\label{eq:intro-corrected}
 \mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_3^{B_0+bH}(E)
 \leq \left(\frac{a^2}{6}+k\right)H^2\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_1^{B_0+bH}(E)
 \qquad(a>0,\ b\in\mathbb R).$$ For the same fixed data, the stronger bound $$\label{eq:intro-tail}
 \mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_3^{B_0+bH}(E)
 \leq \frac{a^2}{6}H^2\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_1^{B_0+bH}(E)$$ holds whenever $a>R_*$, for every $b\in\mathbb R$.*

</div>

There is no canonical-bundle hypothesis in this theorem. Both constants are independent of the rank and Chern character of $E$. The curve class $\Gamma=kH^2\in N_1(X)_{\mathbb Q}$ gives the correction $\Gamma\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_1^{B_0+bH}(E)$ and satisfies $\Gamma\cdot H\geq0$.

Bernardara–Macrì–Schmidt–Zhao proved a corrected inequality of this kind for Fano threefolds polarized by a divisor proportional to $-K_X$, and asked for such a curve-class correction on every polarized threefold (Bernardara et al. 2017, Theorem 1.1 and Question 2.4) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[BMSZ2017](#ref-BMSZ2017)]. Martinez–Schmidt stated the version allowing a fixed rational transverse twist (Martinez and Schmidt 2019, Question 2.6) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[MartinezSchmidt2019](#ref-MartinezSchmidt2019)]. Theorem <a href="#thm:uniform-inequality" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:uniform-inequality">2</a> answers these corrected-inequality questions affirmatively and also gives a volume threshold beyond which the correction can be removed.

The uncorrected inequality at every parameter is false. Schmidt’s blow-up example (Schmidt 2017, Theorem 3.1) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Schmidt2017](#ref-Schmidt2017)] and the contracted-divisor and Weierstrass examples of Martinez–Schmidt (Martinez and Schmidt 2019, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[MartinezSchmidt2019](#ref-MartinezSchmidt2019)] give explicit violations. Section <a href="#sec:counterexamples" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:counterexamples">8</a> computes their bounded volume ranges and proves a uniform bound for semistable sheaves of arbitrary rank on the reduced exceptional surface. These comparisons retain both parts of Theorem <a href="#thm:uniform-inequality" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:uniform-inequality">2</a>: the corrected all-volume statement and the uncorrected tail.

The physical volume and the tilt variable satisfy $t=\sqrt3a$. Consequently the strong bound <a href="#eq:intro-tail" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:intro-tail">[eq:intro-tail]</a> has coefficient $t^2/18$, whereas positivity of the ordinary charge <a href="#eq:intro-physical" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:intro-physical">[eq:intro-physical]</a> on zero-slope objects supplies the weaker coefficient $t^2/2$. The proof of the strong inequality is independent of the construction of the charges and their full numerical support.

The uncorrected tail also gives an all-slope quadratic formulation. For $v=(v_0,v_1,v_2,v_3)$ define <a id="eq:intro-quadratic"></a>$$\label{eq:intro-quadratic}
 Q_{\alpha,\beta}(v)=
 \alpha(v_1^2-2v_0v_2)
 +\beta(3v_0v_3-v_1v_2)+2v_2^2-3v_1v_3.$$

<a id="stmt-cor-quadratic-tail"></a>
<div id="cor:quadratic-tail" class="corollary">

**Corollary 3** (A parabolic region with trivial correction). *For every polarized smooth projective threefold $(X,H)$ there is $R_*>0$ such that, if $$\alpha>f(\beta):=\frac{\beta^2+R_*^2}{2},$$ then $Q_{\alpha,\beta}(v_H(E))\geq0$ for every nonzero semistable object in $\mathcal B_{H,\beta H}$ for the slope $$\frac{v_2-\beta v_1+(\beta^2-\alpha)v_0}{v_1-\beta v_0},
 \qquad
 v_H(E)=(H^3\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_0(E),H^2\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_1(E),H\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_2(E),\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_3(E)),$$ again interpreted as $+\infty$ at zero denominator.*

</div>

The function $f$ is continuous. This is the generalized quadratic formulation with trivial correction: $v_H$ uses the ordinary, unmodified Chern character. The slope in the corollary is exactly <a href="#eq:intro-tilt" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:intro-tilt">[eq:intro-tilt]</a> with $a=\sqrt{2\alpha-\beta^2}$ and $b=\beta$; Section <a href="#sec:wall" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:wall">7</a> proves the all-slope implication, including zero denominator.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Previous constructions

General existence of stability conditions on projective varieties is supplied by Li’s construction (C. Li 2026) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Li2026](#ref-Li2026)] and the framework of Li–Liu–Liu–Macrì–Perry–Stellari–Zhao (C. Li et al. 2026) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[LiEtAl2026](#ref-LiEtAl2026)]. Cheng establishes full numerical support and general nonemptiness in (Cheng 2026) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[Cheng2026](#ref-Cheng2026)]. Thus nonemptiness alone is already known in the setting of Theorem <a href="#thm:geometric-sector" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:geometric-sector">1</a>. Our construction uses the product, restriction and deformation methods of these works to prescribe the two charges and the real sector. The product-embedding route to full numerical support in Section <a href="#sec:ambient" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:ambient">2</a> adapts the construction of Giovenzana–Robotis–Rota–Zuliani (Giovenzana et al. 2026) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[GiovenzanaRobotisRotaZuliani2026](#ref-GiovenzanaRobotisRotaZuliani2026)], distinct from the quantitative product inputs cited there.

There is also a preceding large-volume construction with a different character. Cheng–Feyzbakhsh identify a large-volume double-tilt heart for the character $\mathop{\mathrm{td}}(N_{X/\mathbb P^n})^{-1}\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}$, where $N_{X/\mathbb P^n}$ is the normal bundle of their projective embedding (Cheng and Feyzbakhsh 2026, sec. 3) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[ChengFeyzbakhsh2026](#ref-ChengFeyzbakhsh2026)]. A reparameterization expresses that heart as an ordinary double tilt while leaving a curve-class correction in its charge. The real-parameter extension discussed in their Section 3.3 follows from (C. Li et al. 2026, Theorem 4.1, Proposition 4.5 and Theorem 6.2) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[LiEtAl2026](#ref-LiEtAl2026)]. Combining the mass–Hom estimate (C. Li et al. 2026, Theorem 7.5) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[LiEtAl2026](#ref-LiEtAl2026)] with Cheng’s full-support criterion (Cheng 2026, Theorem 2.1) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[Cheng2026](#ref-Cheng2026)] gives full numerical support for this fixed-polarization real family, pointwise in its parameters. Theorem <a href="#thm:geometric-sector" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:geometric-sector">1</a> prescribes the ordinary and square-root-Todd charges on an open set of real twists and ample directions with one volume threshold. Its heart comparison adapts the surface-section method of Cheng–Feyzbakhsh to the ordinary character and positive weighted sections.

Restriction to lower-dimensional varieties has also led to strong tilt inequalities. Feyzbakhsh–Koseki–Liu–Rekuski derive a corrected tilt inequality from an improved sheaf Bogomolov–Gieseker bound, and obtain that bound on families of Calabi–Yau threefolds from Brill–Noether estimates on curves inside surface sections (Feyzbakhsh et al. 2025, Theorems 1.1, 1.3 and 1.4) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[FeyzbakhshEtAl2025](#ref-FeyzbakhshEtAl2025)]. Our numerical argument likewise uses successive restrictions, but its input is a rank-uniform cohomology estimate on surfaces. A Frobenius limit then gives the inequality at one fixed positive volume on an arbitrary smooth projective threefold. The wall argument builds on the discriminant-descent method of (Bernardara et al. 2017, Lemma 2.7) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[BMSZ2017](#ref-BMSZ2017)]; the fixed-volume defect estimate is what makes its resulting threshold uniform in the twist and the object.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### Two independent proof strategies

We prove the prescribed-charge theorem first, in Sections <a href="#sec:ambient" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:ambient">2</a>–<a href="#sec:heart" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:heart">3</a>, and then establish the strong inequalities in Sections <a href="#sec:tilt" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:tilt">4</a>–<a href="#sec:wall" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:wall">7</a>. Figure <a href="#fig:proof" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:proof">1</a> shows the two arguments. Their distinction is mathematical: controlling four Chern-character degrees along one polarization does not itself control the full numerical group or provide a common threshold when the ample direction varies.

For the charge construction, choose very ample line bundles whose positive span contains the ample directions under consideration. They give an embedding $i:X\hookrightarrow Y$ into a product of projective spaces. In dimension three, products of divisor classes span all numerical Chern-character classes; choosing the line bundles to span $N^1(X)_{\mathbb R}$ therefore makes $i_*$ injective on the numerical Grothendieck group. Ambient support will then control every numerical class on $X$.

The ambient construction must also permit a deformation which is uniform at large volume. Liu’s product-with-a-curve construction (Liu 2021) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[LiuProduct2021](#ref-LiuProduct2021)], the positive-genus product theory of (C. Li et al. 2025) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[ProductsCurves2025](#ref-ProductsCurves2025)], the support induction of (C. Li et al. 2026) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[LiEtAl2026](#ref-LiEtAl2026)], and Cheng’s two-block construction (Cheng 2026) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[Cheng2026](#ref-Cheng2026)] supply its starting framework. We prove a weighted support estimate for finitely many projective blocks after rescaling each Chern-character coordinate by its power of the volume. In these coordinates the support constant is independent of volume, and every fixed positive-degree correction to the exponential charge tends to zero as the volume grows. This permits the ambient deformation throughout an open set of twists and ample directions. A fixed polynomial correction cancels the Todd factors in Grothendieck–Riemann–Roch, so restriction gives exactly the chosen character on $X$.

This construction already gives full numerical support and stable point sheaves. For the ordinary charge, compatible restrictions to smooth surfaces identify its heart. The induced surface hearts control the outer cohomology sheaves; positive weights combine their slope bounds for a real ample direction. A separate argument at the real axis fixes the zero-slope boundary of the second tilt. The Todd-charge construction uses the geometric heart obtained by restriction.

The independent numerical proof is concentrated at one fixed volume $A$. For a slope-zero object $E$ at this volume, put $d=H^2\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_1^B(E)/H^3$ and $r=\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_0(E)$. The tilt discriminant inequality gives $d\geq A|r|$. We prove $$\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_3^B(E)}{H^3}-\frac{A^2d}{6}\le C(d-A|r|)$$ with $C$ independent of $E$ and $b$. The error $e=d-A|r|$ can vanish even at large rank, so the estimates must remain proportional to this error without an additional rank term. Truncating the ordinary slope filtrations separates sheaf factors in a narrow slope interval from a residual complex whose rank and first two intersection degrees are bounded by $e$. Langer’s restriction inequality controls the resulting slope variance (Langer 2004b, 2004a) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[Langer2004](#ref-Langer2004), [Langer2004Addendum](#ref-Langer2004Addendum)]. Hermitian–Einstein theory and the Bochner identity (Donaldson 1985; Uhlenbeck and Yau 1986; Demailly 2012) <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[Donaldson1985](#ref-Donaldson1985), [UhlenbeckYau1986](#ref-UhlenbeckYau1986), [DemaillyCADG](#ref-DemaillyCADG)], together with a matrix spectral estimate of Cwikel–Lieb–Rozenblum type (Frank 2014) <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[Frank2014](#ref-Frank2014)], control the residual cohomology in terms of $e$. For each fixed object, we reduce finitely many sheaves and maps to positive characteristic. Frobenius pullback and Riemann–Roch turn the cohomology bounds into the displayed inequality on $X$; the errors depending on the chosen object vanish in the limit. Transport along zero-slope branches, with strict discriminant descent at walls, gives the corrected global bound and the uncorrected tail.

<figure id="fig:proof">

<figcaption>The independent categorical and numerical arguments. Full support is obtained in Section <a href="#sec:ambient" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:ambient">2</a>, before the ordinary-heart comparison in Section <a href="#sec:heart" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:heart">3</a>. The numerical argument begins with the tilt tools of Section <a href="#sec:tilt" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:tilt">4</a> and proves the inequalities in Sections <a href="#sec:analysis" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:analysis">5</a>–<a href="#sec:wall" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:wall">7</a>. The relation $t=\sqrt3a$ compares volume conventions.</figcaption>
</figure>

For the numerical argument, Section <a href="#sec:tilt" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:tilt">4</a> supplies the circle and duality tools, Sections <a href="#sec:analysis" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:analysis">5</a> and <a href="#sec:apex" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:apex">6</a> establish the fixed-volume bound, and Section <a href="#sec:wall" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:wall">7</a> proves Theorem <a href="#thm:uniform-inequality" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:uniform-inequality">2</a> and Corollary <a href="#cor:quadratic-tail" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:quadratic-tail">3</a>. Section <a href="#sec:counterexamples" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:counterexamples">8</a> compares the inequalities with counterexamples and bounds arbitrary-rank sheaves on reduced exceptional surfaces.

<a id="sec:ambient"></a>
### Prescribed charges on the full numerical lattice

We begin the proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:geometric-sector" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:geometric-sector">1</a> by constructing both prescribed charges with full numerical support. This argument is independent of the strong tilt inequalities. Throughout the section, $X$ is a smooth projective complex threefold with $K_X\simeq\mathcal O_X$.

The construction takes place first on a product of projective spaces. We choose an embedding that retains every numerical class of $X$, prove a support estimate adapted to increasing volume on the product, and use that estimate to deform its charge. A fixed polynomial correction then makes Grothendieck–Riemann–Roch restrict the deformed charge to the chosen charge on $X$. The same construction on smooth surface sections will later identify the ordinary heart. We use numerical Grothendieck groups in the Euler-pairing sense: $$K_{\mathrm{num}}(X)=K_0(X)/\{v:\chi(v,u)=0\text{ for every }u\in K_0(X)\}.$$ The left and right radicals coincide by Serre duality, so either side of the Euler pairing may be used in the adjunction argument below. All real vector spaces associated with these groups are finite-dimensional. We use Bridgeland’s stability conditions and locally finite slicings (Bridgeland 2007) <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[Bridgeland2007](#ref-Bridgeland2007)]. A stability condition has *full numerical support* if, for a norm on $K_{\mathrm{num}}(X)_\mathbb R$, there is a constant $C$ such that $\|[E]\|\le C|Z(E)|$ for every semistable object $E$. The constant may depend on the stability condition. This is equivalent to the quadratic-form definition (Bayer 2019, Definition 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-29"></a>[[BayerDeformation2019](#ref-BayerDeformation2019)]: one may take $C^2|Z(v)|^2-\|v\|^2$, which is negative definite on $\ker Z$.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### An embedding which retains every numerical class

The product-projective embedding and full-support construction here adapt the approach of Giovenzana–Robotis–Rota–Zuliani (Giovenzana et al. 2026) <a id="pm-cite-30"></a>[[GiovenzanaRobotisRotaZuliani2026](#ref-GiovenzanaRobotisRotaZuliani2026)]. The numerical injectivity argument is given below; the quantitative product inputs and the prescribed-charge, real-sector refinements are treated separately in the subsequent subsections.

<a id="stmt-lem-full-numerical-embedding"></a>
<div id="lem:full-numerical-embedding" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:full-numerical-lattice"></a>
**Lemma 4**. *Let $X$ be a smooth projective threefold. Rational Chern characters identify its numerical Grothendieck group with $$\label{eq:full-numerical-lattice}
 K_{\mathrm{num}}(X)_\mathbb Q\simeq
 \mathbb Q\oplus N^1(X)_\mathbb Q\oplus N_1(X)_\mathbb Q\oplus\mathbb Q.$$ Products of numerical divisor classes span this space. Given a real ample class $H_*$, there are very ample line bundles $L_1,\ldots,L_q$ whose classes form a basis of $N^1(X)_\mathbb R$ and positive numbers $s_1,\ldots,s_q$ with $H_*=\sum_hs_hL_h$. For the product embedding <a id="eq:product-embedding"></a>$$\label{eq:product-embedding}
 i:X\hookrightarrow Y=\prod_{h=1}^q\mathbb P^{d_h},
 \qquad d_h=h^0(X,L_h)-1,$$ the pushforward $i_*:K_{\mathrm{num}}(X)_\mathbb R\to K_{\mathrm{num}}(Y)_\mathbb R$ is injective.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-full-numerical-embedding"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-full-numerical-embedding</summary>

*Proof.* The rational Chern character identifies the Grothendieck group with the rational Chow group (The Stacks Project Authors 2026, Tags 0FDI, 0FEW, 0FB2 and 0FB5) <a id="pm-cite-31"></a>[[StacksProject](#ref-StacksProject)]. In Riemann–Roch the Euler pairing is the intersection pairing after applying the invertible operations of dualizing a class and multiplying it by $\mathop{\mathrm{td}}(X)$. Its radical therefore gives precisely the numerical quotient of the Chow group. In dimensions zero and three this quotient is $\mathbb Q$; in the other two dimensions it is the divisor–curve pairing in <a href="#eq:full-numerical-lattice" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:full-numerical-lattice">[eq:full-numerical-lattice]</a>.

For a rational ample class $A$, the Hodge index theorem makes $(D,D')\mapsto ADD'$ nondegenerate on $N^1(X)_\mathbb Q$; see (Greb, Ross, and Toma 2016, Theorem 6.4 and Corollary 6.5) <a id="pm-cite-32"></a>[[GrebRossToma2016](#ref-GrebRossToma2016)] for its real-ample formulation. The divisor–curve pairing is nondegenerate by numerical equivalence, so multiplication by $A$ is an isomorphism $N^1(X)_\mathbb Q\to N_1(X)_\mathbb Q$. Divisors, their products with $A$, and $A^3$ consequently span every summand of <a href="#eq:full-numerical-lattice" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:full-numerical-lattice">[eq:full-numerical-lattice]</a>.

Choose an affine slice transverse to the ray of $H_*$ and a rational simplex in its ample cone whose interior contains that ray. Its $q=\rho(X)$ vertices are linearly independent. Taking sufficiently large integral multiples makes them very ample without changing their positive cone. This also explains the rank-one case, where a single very ample class suffices.

Pushforward is defined on numerical groups by adjunction with perfect complexes. If $i_*v=0$ numerically, the projection formula gives $$\chi(i^*W,v)=\chi(W,i_*v)=0\qquad(W\in K_0(Y)).$$ Chern characters of line bundles on $Y$ span its polynomial Chow ring, and their restrictions span <a href="#eq:full-numerical-lattice" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:full-numerical-lattice">[eq:full-numerical-lattice]</a>. Nondegeneracy of the Euler pairing gives $v=0$. The spanning argument here uses dimension three; it makes no analogous claim in arbitrary dimension. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### Scaled support on elliptic products

The next estimate is the quantitative reason that the ambient charge can be corrected uniformly at large volume. Its coordinates rescale a codimension-$j$ character by the expected power $R^{n-j}$. In these coordinates a fixed positive-codimension correction of the exponential charge will tend to zero as $R\to\infty$. A support bound independent of $R$ then turns this coordinate estimate into a bound on every semistable object.

We first establish the support bound on an elliptic product. The product construction and the uniqueness needed to compare its different orders of induction are external inputs. The estimate below makes their volume scaling explicit.

<a id="stmt-lem-elliptic-scaled-support"></a>
<div id="lem:elliptic-scaled-support" class="lemma">

**Lemma 5** (Scaled support on an elliptic product). *Let $C$ be an elliptic curve, let $n\ge1$, and fix positive rational numbers $\rho_1,\ldots,\rho_n$. For rational $R\ge1$, put $w_r=R\rho_r$ and let $H_r$ be the point-divisor class from the $r$th factor of $C^n$. The product stability condition with charge $$Z_{\mathbf w}(u)=-\int_{C^n}e^{-i\sum_rw_rH_r}\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}(u)$$ has support on the coordinate lattice $$x_I(u)=H_I\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_{n-|I|}(u),\qquad
 H_I=\prod_{r\in I}H_r,\qquad I\subset\{1,\ldots,n\}.$$ More precisely, set $y_I=(\prod_{r\in I}w_r)x_I$. In these coordinates, $$Z_{\mathbf w}(u)=\widehat Z_n(y(u)),\qquad
 \widehat Z_n(y)=-\sum_I(-i)^{|I|}y_I.$$ For a fixed Euclidean norm there is $C_n>0$, independent of $R$, such that $$\|y(u)\|\le C_n|Z_{\mathbf w}(u)|
 \qquad(u\text{ the class of a semistable object}).$$ The constants can be chosen uniformly when the positive ratios $\rho_r$ vary in a compact set. Point sheaves are stable of phase $1$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-elliptic-scaled-support"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-elliptic-scaled-support</summary>

*Proof.* We use the product-with-a-curve construction of (Liu 2021, Lemma 5.7 and Remark 5.8) <a id="pm-cite-33"></a>[[LiuProduct2021](#ref-LiuProduct2021)], the positive-genus product construction of (C. Li et al. 2025, Theorem 4.5) <a id="pm-cite-34"></a>[[ProductsCurves2025](#ref-ProductsCurves2025)], and the support induction of (C. Li et al. 2026, Lemma 6.6) <a id="pm-cite-35"></a>[[LiEtAl2026](#ref-LiEtAl2026)]. For $n=1$, the usual slope stability on $C$ has charge $-x_{\varnothing}+iw_1x_{\{1\}}$ and the assertion is immediate.

Suppose that the scaled support bound has been proved in dimension $n-1$. Omitting factor $r$ splits the coordinates into $x',x''$, where $x'$ consists of those containing $r$, with that index removed. The product construction has charge $$Z_{n-1}(x'')-iw_rZ_{n-1}(x').$$ It initially gives support with respect to its own quotient lattice. Every choice of omitted factor gives the same exponential charge and the same phase $1$ for point sheaves. The uniqueness theorem (C. Li et al. 2025, Theorem 3.11) <a id="pm-cite-36"></a>[[ProductsCurves2025](#ref-ProductsCurves2025)] therefore identifies the slicings: that theorem permits the two support lattices to differ. We may consequently test all the product support forms on the same semistable objects, before proving support on the full coordinate lattice.

Write $M_{\widehat r}$ for scaling the remaining coordinates by their products of weights. The scaled coordinates split as $$y'=w_rM_{\widehat r}x',\qquad y''=M_{\widehat r}x''.$$ The lower-dimensional charge in scaled coordinates is a fixed linear map $\widehat Z$. Increase its support constant $C_{n-1}$ if needed and choose the support form <a id="eq:elliptic-support-form"></a>$$\label{eq:elliptic-support-form}
 q_{n-1}(v)=C_{n-1}^2|\widehat Z(v)|^2-\|v\|^2.$$ It is nonnegative on lower-dimensional semistable classes. Crucially, $$q_{n-1}(v)\le C_{n-1}^2|\widehat Z(v)|^2
 \quad\text{for every }v,$$ not merely for semistable classes. This is the norm form used in the product support estimate.

<a id="eq:weighted-product-form"></a>
For clarity, that estimate has the following coefficient condition. Write $Z_{n-1}(x')=\alpha+i\beta$ and $Z_{n-1}(x'')=\gamma+i\delta$. With equal positive product parameters $s=t=w_r$, the form $$\beta\gamma-\alpha\delta
       +\eta_r q_{n-1}(M_{\widehat r}x')$$ is nonnegative on product-semistable classes when $0\le\eta_r\le w_r/C_{n-1}^2$ (Liu 2021, Lemma 5.7) <a id="pm-cite-37"></a>[[LiuProduct2021](#ref-LiuProduct2021)]; this is the substitution in (C. Li et al. 2026, Lemma 6.6) <a id="pm-cite-38"></a>[[LiEtAl2026](#ref-LiEtAl2026)]. Choose $0<\lambda_r<C_{n-1}^{-2}$, independently of $R$, and put $\eta_r=\lambda_rw_r$. If $$\widehat Z(y')=\widehat\alpha+i\widehat\beta,
 \qquad \widehat Z(y'')=\widehat\gamma+i\widehat\delta,$$ then multiplication of the product form by $w_r$ gives $$\label{eq:weighted-product-form}
 q_r(y)=\widehat\beta\widehat\gamma
       -\widehat\alpha\widehat\delta
       +\lambda_rq_{n-1}(y').$$ All powers of $R$ have disappeared. This form is nonnegative on the common semistable objects just constructed.

The total charge in these coordinates is $(\widehat\gamma+\widehat\beta)
+i(\widehat\delta-\widehat\alpha)$. On its kernel, $$q_r(y)=-|\widehat Z(y')|^2+\lambda_rq_{n-1}(y')
       =(\lambda_rC_{n-1}^2-1)|\widehat Z(y')|^2
           -\lambda_r\|y'\|^2.$$ It is nonpositive there, and vanishes only when $y'=0$; the kernel condition then also gives $\widehat Z(y'')=0$. Sum <a href="#eq:weighted-product-form" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:weighted-product-form">[eq:weighted-product-form]</a> over all omitted factors. If the sum vanishes on the total charge kernel, then $y'=0$ for every factor. This kills every coordinate with $I\ne\varnothing$, and the charge kills the remaining top-degree coordinate. The summed form is thus negative definite on the charge kernel and nonnegative on semistables. Compactness on the unit sphere of its nonnegative cone gives $\|y(u)\|\le C_n|Z_{\mathbf w}(u)|$ for every semistable class $u$. The forms and all choices were made in the scaled coordinates. In particular they are independent of $R$ and can be kept uniform on compact sets of positive ratios. This completes the support induction. The geometricity assertion is part of the positive-genus product construction; see also (C. Li et al. 2026, Theorem 4.1(1)) <a id="pm-cite-39"></a>[[LiEtAl2026](#ref-LiEtAl2026)]. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-10"></a>
#### Transfer to projective blocks

For a slicing $\mathcal R$, denote the largest and smallest Harder–Narasimhan phases of a nonzero object by $\phi^+_{\mathcal R}$ and $\phi^-_{\mathcal R}$. We use the distance $$d(\mathcal R,\mathcal R')=
 \sup_{E\ne0}\max\{
 |\phi^+_{\mathcal R}(E)-\phi^+_{\mathcal R'}(E)|,
 |\phi^-_{\mathcal R}(E)-\phi^-_{\mathcal R'}(E)|\}.$$ For a line bundle $L$ and an integer $k$, we say that $\mathcal R$ has the *Bayer property* for $(L,k)$ if $F\otimes L[k]$ has all phases at least $\phi$ whenever $F\in\mathcal R(\phi)$. We will use both a support bound and a small phase change under each fixed line-bundle twist. They are inherited from an elliptic product through finite quotients.

<a id="stmt-prop-weighted-ambient"></a>
<div id="prop:weighted-ambient" class="proposition">

<a id="eq:ambient-scaled-coordinates"></a>
<a id="eq:ambient-support"></a>
<a id="eq:ambient-twist-distance"></a>
**Proposition 6**. *Let $Y=\prod_{h=1}^q\mathbb P^{d_h}$, put $\xi_h=c_1(\mathcal O_h(1))$, and fix $s_h>0$. There is a continuous family of numerical stability conditions $(U_R,\mathcal R_R)$ for $R\ge1$ with $$U_R(u)=-\int_Ye^{-iR\sum_hs_h\xi_h}\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}(u).$$ All point sheaves are stable of phase $1$. Write $\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}(u)=\sum_{\mathbf j}u_{\mathbf j}\xi^{\mathbf j}$, where $0\le j_h\le d_h$, and set $$\label{eq:ambient-scaled-coordinates}
 (M_Ru)_{\mathbf j}=
 u_{\mathbf j}\prod_h(Rs_h)^{d_h-j_h}.$$ For a fixed Euclidean norm there is $D>0$, independent of $R$, such that $$\label{eq:ambient-support}
 \|M_Ru\|\le D|U_R(u)|\qquad
 (u\text{ the class of an }\mathcal R_R\text{-semistable object}).$$ For every $\mathbf e\in\mathbb Z^q$, $$\label{eq:ambient-twist-distance}
 d(\mathcal R_R,\mathcal R_R\otimes\mathcal O_Y(\mathbf e))
 \le\sum_h\frac{d_h|e_h|}{\pi Rs_h}.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-weighted-ambient"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-weighted-ambient</summary>

*Proof.* Cheng proves the two-block construction and phase estimate in (Cheng 2026, Proposition 4.3 and Remark 4.4) <a id="pm-cite-40"></a>[[Cheng2026](#ref-Cheng2026)]. We apply the same quotient construction block by block, using Lemma <a href="#lem:elliptic-scaled-support" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:elliptic-scaled-support">5</a> to control all coordinates uniformly in the volume.

First take $R$ and the positive weights $s_h$ rational. Put $n=\sum_hd_h$ and give each of the $d_h$ elliptic factors in block $h$ the weight $w_r=2Rs_h$. The lemma supplies the starting stability condition and its scaled support bound. Only the coordinate lattice of the elliptic power is used here; full numerical support will follow on $Y$ from the explicit pullback calculation below.

First apply (C. Li et al. 2026, Proposition 5.1) <a id="pm-cite-41"></a>[[LiEtAl2026](#ref-LiEtAl2026)] to the stability condition just constructed on the coordinate lattice. It supplies the descent by $(\mathbb Z/2)^n$ to $(\mathbb P^1)^n$ and the Bayer property with $k=0$ for every effective line bundle $\mathcal O(a_1,\ldots,a_n)$, with all $a_r\ge0$. We then take the symmetric quotients one block at a time.

Here is the filtration used at a symmetric step. For $Q=(\mathbb P^1)^d\to\mathbb P^d$, there is a finite filtration of $\mathcal O_{Q\times_{\mathbb P^d}Q}$ whose successive quotients are $$p_2^*\mathcal L_j^{-1}\otimes\mathcal O_{\Gamma(g_j)},
 \qquad g_j\in S_d,\qquad
 \mathcal L_j=\mathcal O(a_{j1},\ldots,a_{jd}),\quad a_{jr}\ge0,$$ where $p_2$ is the second projection and $\Gamma(g_j)$ is the graph of the permutation $g_j$ (C. Li et al. 2026, Definition 3.19 and Example 3.20(3)) <a id="pm-cite-42"></a>[[LiEtAl2026](#ref-LiEtAl2026)]. At a block stage the map is $f:Q\times M\to\mathbb P^d\times M$, where $M$ is the product of the other blocks, and its fiber square is $(Q\times_{\mathbb P^d}Q)\times M$. Flat pullback to this product preserves the filtration and its exact sequences. Its graph automorphisms are $g_j\times\operatorname{id}_M$, and its line bundles remain pulled back from $Q$.

The finite-flat descent criterion (C. Li et al. 2026, Proposition 3.21) <a id="pm-cite-43"></a>[[LiEtAl2026](#ref-LiEtAl2026)] therefore requires precisely invariance under these permutations and the Bayer properties with $k=0$ for these effective line bundles. Indeed, for a phase-$\phi$ object $F$, the factors of $f^*f_*F$ are $\mathcal L_j^{-1}\otimes g_{j*}F$; the two properties put their largest phases at most $\phi$. On the elliptic product, signs and permutations within each equal-weight block preserve the charge and point phases, so the uniqueness theorem used above gives their invariance. Block permutations normalize the full sign group; their invariance descends through the completed involution quotient. For subsequent block quotients, the required properties pass to the remaining blocks as follows (C. Li et al. 2026, Lemma 3.9 and Remark 3.14(3)) <a id="pm-cite-44"></a>[[LiEtAl2026](#ref-LiEtAl2026)]. At each quotient $f:V\to V'$, pullback computes extremal phases for the induced slicing $\mathcal S'=f_\#\mathcal S$. If a line bundle $L$ comes from an unquotiented block, then $$\phi^-_{\mathcal S'}(F\otimes L[k])
 =\phi^-_{\mathcal S}(f^*F\otimes f^*L[k]).$$ The Bayer property for $f^*L$ therefore descends to $L$. An automorphism of another block commutes with $f$, so its invariance descends by the same pullback description. Thus every remaining quotient has the required Bayer properties and invariance, for any finite number of blocks.

For completeness, let $g$ be the resulting map from the elliptic power to $Y$. Pullback satisfies $$g^*\xi_h^{j_h}=2^{j_h}j_h!
       \sum_{|J_h|=j_h}H_{J_h},
 \qquad \deg g=2^n\prod_hd_h!.$$ If $|I\cap\text{block }h|=d_h-j_h$, then the scaled $I$-coordinate of $g^*u$ is <a id="eq:block-coordinate-comparison"></a>$$\label{eq:block-coordinate-comparison}
 \Bigl(\prod_{r\in I}w_r\Bigr)
 H_I\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_{n-|I|}(g^*u)
 =2^n\Bigl(\prod_hj_h!\Bigr)(M_Ru)_{\mathbf j}.$$ The fixed multiplicities are $\prod_h\binom{d_h}{j_h}$. Pullback is thus injective on the complete Chern-character lattice of $Y$, with norm comparison constants independent of $R$. Semistability pulls back, and $Z_{\mathbf w}(g^*u)=\deg(g)U_R(u)$. This proves <a href="#eq:ambient-support" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ambient-support">[eq:ambient-support]</a>.

For the phase estimate, vary the real tensor parameter on each elliptic factor successively. The one-factor argument in the proof of (Cheng and Feyzbakhsh 2026, Theorem 2.4) <a id="pm-cite-45"></a>[[ChengFeyzbakhsh2026](#ref-ChengFeyzbakhsh2026)], used in (Cheng 2026, Remark 4.4) <a id="pm-cite-46"></a>[[Cheng2026](#ref-Cheng2026)], gives its absolute variation divided by $\pi w_r$. A twist $e_h\xi_h$ pulls back with coefficient $2e_h$ on the $d_h$ factors in block $h$; its factor $2$ cancels the factor $2$ in $w_r$. Adding the variations gives <a href="#eq:ambient-twist-distance" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ambient-twist-distance">[eq:ambient-twist-distance]</a>. Finally the deformation and multiplication-isogeny argument in the proof of (Cheng 2026, Proposition 4.3) <a id="pm-cite-47"></a>[[Cheng2026](#ref-Cheng2026)] extends the rational-weight construction continuously to positive real weights. It applies factor by factor: the scaled support forms just constructed remain fixed on compact positive weight sets, the exponential charges vary continuously, and uniqueness on overlapping deformation neighborhoods identifies the lifts. The closed support inequalities and the phase bounds persist. This also extends $R$ to all real $R\ge1$. Point stability in this extension follows separately from (C. Li et al. 2026, Theorem 4.1(1)) <a id="pm-cite-48"></a>[[LiEtAl2026](#ref-LiEtAl2026)] on the elliptic power and preservation of geometricity under finite pushforward (C. Li et al. 2026, Remark 3.11) <a id="pm-cite-49"></a>[[LiEtAl2026](#ref-LiEtAl2026)]. Concretely, for the finite faithfully flat quotient $g$, the sheaf $g^*\mathcal O_y$ has a finite filtration by point sheaves, all stable of phase $1$. A phase-one subobject and quotient of $\mathcal O_y$ pull back to objects whose Jordan–Hölder factors are among these points, so both pullbacks are coherent finite-length sheaves. Faithful flatness descends this cohomological concentration. The original subobject and quotient are therefore sheaves, and simplicity of $\mathcal O_y$ rules out a proper nonzero subobject. Continuity and the charge value $-1$ normalize the point phase to $1$. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-11"></a>
#### Geometric slicings and the restriction criterion

We now prepare the restriction step. The first consequence of point stability below gives the cohomological bounds needed for a truncated resolution. The second will keep points stable during deformation in all ambient charges. Both will also be used to identify the induced heart. Write $\mathcal R(I)$ for the extension-closed category generated by semistable objects with phases in an interval $I$.

<a id="stmt-lem-geometric-range"></a>
<div id="lem:geometric-range" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:geometric-aisles"></a>
**Lemma 7** (Cohomological bounds from points). *Let $V$ be a smooth projective variety of dimension $n\ge2$, and let $\mathcal R$ be a locally finite slicing in which every point sheaf is stable of phase $1$. Then <a id="eq:geometric-range"></a>$$\begin{aligned}
 \mathcal R(0,1]&\subset D^{[-n+1,0]}(V),
 &\mathop{\mathrm{Coh}}(V)&\subset\mathcal R(1-n,1],\label{eq:geometric-range}\\
 D^{\le-n}(V)&\subset\mathcal R(>0),
 &\mathcal R(>0)&\subset D^{\le0}(V).\label{eq:geometric-aisles}
\end{aligned}$$ The sheaf $\mathcal H^{-n+1}(E)$ is torsion-free for $E\in\mathcal R(0,1]$. Moreover, $\mathcal H^0(E)$ is zero-dimensional for $E\in\mathcal R(1)$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-geometric-range"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-geometric-range</summary>

*Proof.* This is the point-object argument of (Bridgeland 2008, Lemma 10.1) <a id="pm-cite-50"></a>[[BridgelandK3](#ref-BridgelandK3)]; we include it to specify the dimension and boundary statements being used. Let $E$ be stable with phase $0<\phi\le1$ and not a point of phase $1$. Phase vanishing gives $\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(E,\mathcal O_x[j])=0$ for $j<0$. For $j\ge n$, Serre duality identifies this space with $\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathcal O_x,E[n-j])^*$, which is zero by phases. At $j=n,\phi=1$, use nonisomorphism of the two stable objects. The canonical bundle causes no change, since its restriction to a point is trivial. A minimal free complex at each closed point consequently has nonzero terms only in degrees $[-n+1,0]$. Its bottom cohomology is a subsheaf of a locally free sheaf and hence is torsion-free. At phase $1$ we also have $\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(E,\mathcal O_x)=0$ for every $x$, so $\mathcal H^0(E)=0$. Point sheaves themselves satisfy the stated cohomological bounds. Finite Jordan–Hölder filtrations and extensions give these assertions for the whole heart; in phase $1$ its zeroth cohomology is an extension of sheaves supported at finitely many points.

A coherent sheaf cannot receive a nonzero morphism from an object whose phase is greater than $1$, by the cohomological bound just proved and a shift. It cannot map nontrivially to an object of phase at most $1-n$: such an object is in $D^{\ge1}$, including the endpoint by the phase-one observation. Applied to its extreme Harder–Narasimhan factors, these vanishings prove $\mathop{\mathrm{Coh}}(V)\subset\mathcal R(1-n,1]$. Shifting this inclusion and the heart bound proves <a href="#eq:geometric-aisles" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:geometric-aisles">[eq:geometric-aisles]</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-lem-primitive-point"></a>
<div id="lem:primitive-point" class="lemma">

**Lemma 8** (Stability from an open set of charges). *Let a stability condition have support on a finite-rank lattice $\Lambda$. Suppose that an object $E$ has primitive class in $\Lambda$ and remains semistable throughout a neighborhood which is open in all central charges $\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(\Lambda,\mathbb C)$. Then $E$ is stable.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-primitive-point"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-primitive-point</summary>

*Proof.* If $E$ is strictly semistable, take a first stable subobject $G$ in a finite Jordan–Hölder filtration. Its class cannot be real proportional to $[E]$. Indeed their aligned charges would make $[G]=c[E]$ with $0<c<1$, whereas primitivity makes an integral class on this line an integral multiple of $[E]$. Choose a phase-window heart of length one centered at their common phase. The triangle $G\to E\to E/G$ remains a short exact sequence in this heart for small deformations: all three objects stay strictly inside its phase window. An independent small perturbation of the two charge values makes the phase of $G$ greater than that of $E$. This contradicts the assumed semistability of $E$ in the entire neighborhood. ◻

</details>

<a id="eq:restriction-hypotheses"></a>
<a id="eq:induced-slicing"></a>
Here is the precise restriction input. Let $f:V\to Y$ be a finite map. For the truncated cofiltrations used below, the data consist of a finite tower in $D^{\mathrm b}(Y)$ $$E_0=f_*\mathcal O_V\longrightarrow E_1\longrightarrow\cdots
      \longrightarrow E_N$$ and distinguished triangles $$P_j\longrightarrow E_j\longrightarrow E_{j+1}
      \longrightarrow P_j[1],\qquad 0\le j<N.$$ Each $P_j$ is a finite direct sum of specified line bundles with specified shifts $L_i[k_i]$, and the remainder satisfies $E_N\in D^{\le-N}(Y)$. Thus the successive cones of the tower are $P_j[1]$; equivalently, $f_*\mathcal O_V$ is built by extensions from the $P_j$ and the remainder. For a truncated resolution, $P_j$ is its $j$th sheaf term shifted by $[j]$ and $E_N$ is the last kernel shifted by $[N]$. The finite-map restriction criterion (C. Li et al. 2026, Proposition 3.24) <a id="pm-cite-51"></a>[[LiEtAl2026](#ref-LiEtAl2026)], extending (Polishchuk 2007, Corollary 2.2.2) <a id="pm-cite-52"></a>[[Polishchuk2007](#ref-Polishchuk2007)], applies to $f$ with this cofiltration. It requires $$\label{eq:restriction-hypotheses}
 \begin{gathered}
 \text{the Bayer properties for every }(L_i,k_i),\\
 D^{\le n_1}(Y)\subset\mathcal R(>0)\subset D^{\le n_2}(Y),
 \qquad N\ge n_2-n_1+1.
 \end{gathered}$$ An untruncated cofiltration has no last numerical requirement. All tensor products in this criterion are derived. The conclusion is a slicing $$\label{eq:induced-slicing}
 f^\sharp\mathcal R(\phi)=
       \{F\in D^{\mathrm b}(V):f_*F\in\mathcal R(\phi)\},$$ with charge $U\circ f_*$ and support on the ambient lattice through $f_*$. In particular it provides the required Harder–Narasimhan filtrations. No surjectivity assumption is imposed on $f$. Smoothness makes the bounded finite-Tor-dimension formulation applicable here. We shall verify the cofiltration and every bound in <a href="#eq:restriction-hypotheses" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:restriction-hypotheses">[eq:restriction-hypotheses]</a> before using the criterion.

<a id="pm-h-12"></a>
#### Correcting the charge before restriction

<a id="eq:two-character-factors"></a>
<a id="eq:gamma-lift"></a>
Fix real classes $B_*,H_*$ with $H_*$ ample, and choose the embedding in Lemma <a href="#lem:full-numerical-embedding" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:full-numerical-embedding">4</a>. We write $L_h$ also for its first Chern class, so $i^*\xi_h=L_h$ and $H_*=\sum_hs_hL_h$ with $s_h>0$. Consider separately the factors $$\label{eq:two-character-factors}
 \Theta=1\qquad\text{and}\qquad
 \Theta=\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{td}}(X)}=1+\frac{c_2(X)}{24}.$$ The assumption on the canonical bundle gives $\mathop{\mathrm{td}}(X)=1+c_2(X)/12$ in the degrees relevant on $X$. By Lemma <a href="#lem:full-numerical-embedding" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:full-numerical-embedding">4</a>, choose a degree-two polynomial $\delta$ in the $\xi_h$ with $i^*\delta=c_2(X)$ numerically. A suitable polynomial on $Y$ with constant term one is $$\gamma=\begin{cases}
 \mathop{\mathrm{td}}(Y)(1-\delta/12),&\Theta=1,\\
 \mathop{\mathrm{td}}(Y)(1-\delta/24),&\Theta=\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{td}}(X)}.
 \end{cases}$$ Products are taken in the finite-dimensional numerical Chow ring. Thus $$\label{eq:gamma-lift}
 i^*\bigl(\gamma/\mathop{\mathrm{td}}(Y)\bigr)\mathop{\mathrm{td}}(X)=\Theta.$$ In particular the two choices remove different Todd contributions.

<a id="eq:deformed-ambient-charge"></a>
<a id="eq:exact-restricted-charge"></a>
Write $B=\sum_hb_hL_h$ and $H=\sum_h\ell_hL_h$. Deform the ambient charge to $$\label{eq:deformed-ambient-charge}
 U_t^{B,H}(u)=-\int_Y
 e^{-\sum_hb_h\xi_h-it\sum_h\ell_h\xi_h}\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}(u)\gamma.$$ Grothendieck–Riemann–Roch and <a href="#eq:gamma-lift" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:gamma-lift">[eq:gamma-lift]</a> give the exact identity $$\label{eq:exact-restricted-charge}
 U_t^{B,H}(i_*E)=-\int_Xe^{-B-itH}\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}(E)\Theta.$$ Numerical equality in <a href="#eq:gamma-lift" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:gamma-lift">[eq:gamma-lift]</a> suffices because only intersection degrees occur. For $\Theta=1$ this is exactly $Z_{B,tH}$; for the other choice it is exactly $Z^{\mathrm{LV}}_{B,tH}$.

<a id="stmt-lem-uniform-ambient-deformation"></a>
<div id="lem:uniform-ambient-deformation" class="lemma">

**Lemma 9**. *For every $0<\epsilon<1/8$, there are open neighborhoods $V\ni B_*$ and $W\ni H_*$ in the real divisor and ample spaces, and $T\ge1$, such that for all $B\in V$, $H\in W$, and $t\ge T$, the charge <a href="#eq:deformed-ambient-charge" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:deformed-ambient-charge">[eq:deformed-ambient-charge]</a> lifts to a stability condition with slicing at distance less than $\epsilon$ from $\mathcal R_t$. The construction is available in an open neighborhood of that charge in the full space $\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(K_{\mathrm{num}}(Y),\mathbb C)$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-uniform-ambient-deformation"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-uniform-ambient-deformation</summary>

<a id="eq:uniform-deformation-estimate"></a>
*Proof.* Take $V$ bounded in the $b_h$ coordinates, and restrict $W$ by $|\ell_h/s_h-1|<\eta$. In the scaled coordinates <a href="#eq:ambient-scaled-coordinates" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ambient-scaled-coordinates">[eq:ambient-scaled-coordinates]</a>, expansion of the two exponentials gives $$\label{eq:uniform-deformation-estimate}
 \bigl\|(U_t^{B,H}-U_t)\circ M_t^{-1}\bigr\|
       \le C_1\eta+C_2t^{-1}.$$ To see the uniformity, the coefficient of $u_{\mathbf j}$ in the uncorrected exponential has degree $n-|\mathbf j|$ in $t$. Varying the positive coefficients $s_h$ to $\ell_h$ changes its ratio to the weight of $M_t$ by $O(\eta)$. A codimension-$d>0$ term of $e^{-\sum b_h\xi_h}\gamma-1$ lowers that degree by $d$ and hence contributes $O(t^{-d})$. There are finitely many monomials, and their coefficients are bounded on the chosen neighborhoods. This proves <a href="#eq:uniform-deformation-estimate" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:uniform-deformation-estimate">[eq:uniform-deformation-estimate]</a> independently of the object.

Combine it with <a href="#eq:ambient-support" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ambient-support">[eq:ambient-support]</a>. Choose $\eta$ first and then $T$ so that $$D(C_1\eta+C_2/T)<\sin(\pi\epsilon).$$ To check the support hypothesis of the deformation theorem explicitly, put $$Q_t(u)=D^2|U_t(u)|^2-\|M_tu\|^2.$$ It is nonnegative on the original semistable classes. If $U'(u)=0$ and $\|(U'-U_t)M_t^{-1}\|\le\delta<1/D$, then $$Q_t(u)\le(D^2\delta^2-1)\|M_tu\|^2<0\qquad(u\ne0).$$ Thus $Q_t$ is negative definite on every charge kernel along the straight deformation, and throughout the indicated open charge region. The support-property deformation theorem (Bayer 2019, Theorem 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-53"></a>[[BayerDeformation2019](#ref-BayerDeformation2019)] lifts the straight path while preserving $Q_t$. On its nonnegative cone, $\|M_tu\|\le D|U_t(u)|$, so the relative charge change is less than $\sin(\pi\epsilon)$. The quantitative phase estimate (Bayer 2019, Lemma 2.9) <a id="pm-cite-54"></a>[[BayerDeformation2019](#ref-BayerDeformation2019)] then makes the slicing distance less than $\epsilon$ as long as it is less than $1/4$. It is zero at the start; continuity along the path and $\epsilon<1/8$ prevent a first exit from that $1/4$ neighborhood. This proves the asserted strict distance bound. The charge bound is strict, so for each fixed $t$ it also holds on an open neighborhood in *all* ambient numerical charges. This is the space on which we deform, before applying restriction. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-prop-geometric-prescribed-charges"></a>
<div id="prop:geometric-prescribed-charges" class="proposition">

**Proposition 10**. *For either factor in <a href="#eq:two-character-factors" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:two-character-factors">[eq:two-character-factors]</a>, the choices in Lemma <a href="#lem:uniform-ambient-deformation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:uniform-ambient-deformation">9</a> can be made so that every $B\in V,H\in W,t\ge T$ gives a locally finite geometric stability condition on $X$, with charge <a href="#eq:exact-restricted-charge" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:exact-restricted-charge">[eq:exact-restricted-charge]</a> and full numerical support. Every point sheaf is stable of phase $1$. For $\Theta=1$, the construction also gives compatible geometric slicings on every smooth section $S_h\in|L_h|$, with Harder–Narasimhan phases computed by pushforward to $Y$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-geometric-prescribed-charges"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-geometric-prescribed-charges</summary>

*Proof.* Set $A=\mathcal O_Y(1,\ldots,1)$. Choose a locally free resolution $P^\bullet\to i_*\mathcal O_X$ with $P^0=\mathcal O_Y$ and with each $P^{-k}$ a finite sum of line bundles $A^{-b}$ for $k\ge1$. Such a resolution is obtained by repeatedly generating the successive coherent kernels after sufficiently positive twists. Fix $N>\dim Y+1$ and truncate after the terms in degrees $0,\ldots,-(N-1)$. The remaining kernel is shifted by $[N]$, so the cofiltration remainder lies in $D^{\le-N}(Y)$.

For a defining ambient linear form of a smooth section $S_h$, first form the cone of the untruncated map $P^\bullet(-\xi_h)\to P^\bullet$. It resolves $(i\circ j_h)_*\mathcal O_{S_h}$, and its term in degree $-k$ is $$P^{-k}\oplus P^{1-k}(-\xi_h),\qquad P^1=0.$$ Truncate each cone resolution after its terms in degrees $0,\ldots,-(N-1)$. Its remaining kernel is again shifted by $[N]$, so its cofiltration remainder also lies in $D^{\le-N}(Y)$. The line bundles in all these terms are independent of the chosen smooth section. Include all their summands, in particular the mixed twists $A^{-b}\otimes\mathcal O_Y(-\xi_h)$, together with $A$ and $\mathcal O_Y(\xi_h)$, in one finite list. This list is fixed before the parameters $B,H,t$ and the sections are chosen.

<a id="eq:deformed-twist-distance"></a>
Let $\mathcal R$ be a lift from Lemma <a href="#lem:uniform-ambient-deformation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:uniform-ambient-deformation">9</a>. Tensoring is an isometry for slicing distance, so <a href="#eq:ambient-twist-distance" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ambient-twist-distance">[eq:ambient-twist-distance]</a> gives, after increasing the single threshold $T$, $$\label{eq:deformed-twist-distance}
 d(\mathcal R,\mathcal R\otimes L)<1$$ for every line bundle $L$ in the finite list, uniformly in the claimed sector. The triangle inequality bounds the left side by $2\epsilon+O(t^{-1})$. The same strict inequalities hold in a full open neighborhood of each lifted charge.

In particular tensoring by $A^{-1}[1]$ has the Bayer property. (C. Li et al. 2026, Proposition 3.27) <a id="pm-cite-55"></a>[[LiEtAl2026](#ref-LiEtAl2026)] makes every point sheaf on $Y$ semistable throughout that open neighborhood. Its numerical class is primitive, because $\chi(\mathcal O_Y,\mathcal O_y)=1$. Lemma <a href="#lem:primitive-point" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:primitive-point">8</a> upgrades semistability to stability. At the charge <a href="#eq:deformed-ambient-charge" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:deformed-ambient-charge">[eq:deformed-ambient-charge]</a> its value is $-1$, and closeness to $\mathcal R_t$ fixes its phase to be $1$.
